Marion Cotillard Says ‘I’m Not Here to Judge Movies’ as Marrakech Jury President and ‘Doesn’t Want Any Information’ Before Watching: ‘Like a Virgin’

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Marion Cotillard, who serves as the jury president at this year’s Marrakech Film Festival, says she prefers watching films with “as little information as possible.”

“I don’t know anything about the movies we’re gonna see, I haven’t seen a single image,” the Oscar-winning French actress said at the festival’s jury press conference on Saturday morning. “That’s how I love to see movies, I don’t want to have any information.” Related Stories Laura Poitras Turns Lens on ICE's 'Paramilitary Occupation' of Minneapolis in 'They’re Here' Ji.hlava New Visions Award Winner Karim Akalay Mixes Magic With ‘Anti-Social Realism’ in ‘6TM’: ‘It’s About Collective Strength Rather Than One Hero’

“I want to be like a virgin,” she quipped.

Ironically, she said she doesn’t think her role on the jury is to “judge movies.” “I consider myself as part of an audience: I’m here to be touched, to be taken by stories.”

Cotillard was also asked about “Inception,” in which she starred alongside Leonardo DiCaprio. Does she understand Christopher Nolan’s famously complex film? “I don’t know…I thought I had,” she said to laughter. “Maybe I have. And maybe I haven’t.”

The nine-member jury includes “Sex Education” and “Barbie” star Emma Mackey; “Phantom Thread” standout Vicky Krieps; Danish actor Claes Bang, who featured in Ruben Östlund’s Palme d’Or winner “The Square”; French actor-turned-director Gilles Lellouche, known for “Beating Hearts”; “The Voice of Hind Rajab” helmer Kaouther Ben Hania; “All of Us Strangers” filmmaker Andrew Haigh; Franco-Senegalese helmer Ramata-Toulaye Sy; and Moroccan director-producer Saïd Hamich Benlarbi.

The jury will award the festival’s Étoile d’Or to one of the 14 films in competition, which consists of first and second features.

Cotillard said her only expectation she has in helping select this year’s winner is “to be shaken, to be moved.”

“When I can watch a movie and I can laugh and smile and be questioned at the same time,” she continued. “When there’s a story that on the paper I wouldn’t be very interested [in] and the filmmaker who tells the story will open a new vision for me.”

She summed it up simply: “How do you judge art anyway?”

Mackey agrees with Cotillard that her role on the jury is to “receive the films as a spectator. “We’re here to learn, its very pure somehow,” the “Sex Education” star said. “I don’t pretend that I know everything and I’m here to learn and to absorb these films as as a cinema lover.”

“It’s an honor and I’m happy to spend a week watching two films a day, that’s amazing,” she added.

Jury members were also asked about the relatively strong female representation at Marrakech and its broader implications for gender equality in the industry. This year, women hold a majority of seats on the nine-member jury while six of the 14 films in competition were directed by women.

Krieps pointed to her role as an Irish maid in Ryan Murphy’s “Monster: The Lizzie Borden Story” to illustrate the importance of female representation on screen. “I think women have had the role of oppressed and silent,” she said. “Often we had to hide, we had to disappear because otherwise we weren’t safe.”

“I think the role I play in ‘Monster’ is someone who becomes very visible in a time when people were supposed to be invisible,” she continued, adding that her character’s wild red hair and contemporary costuming could “help people see differently in the future.”

Sy, meanwhile, offered a more succinct response, suggesting that true progress will come when female representation is no longer a point of discussion.

“The day when we stop counting how many women there are in juries, in jobs, as directors, then it will be normal,” the Sengalese filmmaker said to enthusiastic applause. “We have to stop asking the question and counting how many there are.”

On Friday night, the festival opened with Jesse Eisenberg’s “The Debut,” a musical comedy starring Julianne Moore as a shy New Jersey housewife who auditions for a regional production. Though Eisenberg and Moore weren’t in attendance, the opening ceremony was still a starry affair with guests including Tilda Swinton and “Knives Out” director Rian Johnson, who is giving a talk at the festival on Sunday.

Mackey is next playing the White Witch in Greta Gerwig’s “Narnia: The Magician’s Nephew,” and told Variety on the red carpet that she was excited to meet Swinton — who played the role in the original film trilogy — for the first time at Marrakech.

“It’s thrilling, my absolute dream person,” she said, adding: “She’s the queen. I wouldn’t even know how to breathe around her, I don’t think.”

The Marrakech Film Festival runs through Nov. 28.

Amex Blue Cash Preferred Review: A Top Cash Back Card For Everyday Purchases

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Travel  |  Read Source →

If you spend a lot at supermarkets and want to earn cash back, the Blue Cash Preferred® Card from American Express is hard to beat. The card offers 6% cash back at U.S. supermarkets and on select streaming subscriptions, which can add up fast for families and anyone with a few monthly subscriptions.

In this post, I’ll cover the welcome offer, the bonus categories and how they work, the Disney streaming credit, and how this card compares to other options like the no annual fee Blue Cash Everyday® Card from American Express and the Citi Strata Premier® Card.

Link: Learn more about the Blue Cash Preferred® Card from American Express

Before I get into the Amex Blue Cash Preferred Card review, here’s a quick look at the most important features and fees:

And here are my thoughts on the main pros and cons of the Blue Cash Preferred Card:

When applying for a credit card, obviously the welcome offer, the eligibility, and the fees, are major considerations, given that they can be the first factors that impact interest in a card. So along those lines, let’s take a look at those details for the Amex Blue Cash Preferred.

The Amex Blue Cash Preferred is currently offering the following welcome bonus: You may be eligible for as high as $300 cash back after spending $3,000 in purchases on your new Card in the first 6 months. Welcome offers vary and you may not be eligible for an offer. Cash back is received as Reward Dollars, redeemable for statement credit or for other select options. Terms Apply.

That’s a solid bonus for a card at this price point, and the six month window gives you plenty of time to hit the spending requirement. Welcome offers vary and you may not be eligible for an offer.

Amex cards tend to be fairly easy to get approved for if you have excellent credit. A few rules to know:

As you can see, this card is marketed as offering a bonus “as high as” a certain amount. The way this works, you can apply for the Amex Blue Cash Preferred Card, and then find out your offer amount. Then you can decide whether you want to accept the offer or not. That’s an unconventional approach for a welcome offer. At least Amex has the “apply with confidence” feature, so there won’t be a hard pull unless you accept the offer.

The Amex Blue Cash Preferred has a $0 intro annual fee for the first year, then $95. (Rates & Fees). That puts it in line with other mid-range cards. No first year annual fee is a nice perk, as it lets you test the card and see how much value you’re getting before you commit to the ongoing fee.

If you decide the card isn’t working for you after the first year, you can always downgrade to the no annual fee Blue Cash Everyday Card.

You can add authorized users to the card at no extra cost, so that their spending can help you rack up rewards.

I wouldn’t recommend using the Amex Blue Cash Preferred Card outside the U.S. or for purchases in foreign currencies. The 2.7% foreign transaction fee will eat into your rewards (Rates & Fees). There are plenty of other cards with no foreign transaction fees that are better suited for international spending.

The Amex Blue Cash Preferred has some excellent bonus categories, especially if you spend a lot at U.S. supermarkets and on streaming. Below is a full breakdown.

This is the headline feature of the Amex Blue Cash Preferred, and it’s the single best cash back rate for U.S. supermarket spending that I’m aware of. You earn 6% cash back at U.S. supermarkets on up to $6,000 per year in purchases (then 1%). This is the only bonus category with an earning cap.

To put that in perspective, if you spend $500 a month on U.S. supermarket spending, you’d earn $360 per year in cash back from this category alone. That more than covers the $95 annual fee.

Note that supermarkets don’t include superstores, warehouse clubs (like Costco or Sam’s Club), or discount stores. If most of your grocery shopping happens at a traditional supermarket, this card is incredibly rewarding.

The Amex Blue Cash Preferred also offers 6% cash back on select U.S. streaming subscriptions. The list of qualifying services is quite long and includes most of the major platforms: Netflix, Hulu, Disney+, ESPN+, HBO Max, Spotify, Apple TV, Apple Music, Amazon Music Unlimited, YouTube Premium, YouTube TV, Peacock, Sling TV, and many more.

If you’re spending $50 to $100 a month across streaming services (which many households are these days), that 6% adds up nicely.

The Amex Blue Cash Preferred offers 3% cash back on transit purchases, including taxis, ridesharing, parking, tolls, trains, and buses. If you commute by public transit or use rideshare services regularly, this is a solid bonus category.

With the Amex Blue Cash Preferred, you also earn 3% cash back at eligible U.S. gas stations, with no caps. Note that this doesn’t include gas purchased at superstores, supermarkets, or warehouse clubs that sell fuel.

For purchases that don’t fall into the bonus categories, you earn 1% cash back. This is the one weak spot of the card. At just 1% on non-bonused spending, you’ll want to pair this card with something that earns a better return on everyday purchases (more on that below).

With the Amex Blue Cash Preferred, you earn Reward Dollars that can be redeemed as statement credits, and there’s no minimum cash back balance required before you can redeem. It’s simple and straightforward, though it’s worth knowing that you can’t use cash back to pay your minimum payment due.

As is the case for any credit card, it’s also important to consider the benefits and credits offered, if any. The Amex Blue Cash Preferred has a reasonable annual fee, so what are the benefits like? Well, they’re potentially pretty solid, and can help justify the annual fee…

The Amex Blue Cash Preferred offers up to $10 back per month ($120 per year) as a statement credit when you use your enrolled card to purchase a subscription, including a bundle subscription, on DisneyPlus.com, Hulu.com, or Stream.ESPN.com U.S. websites, with no minimum purchase requirement. Subscription is subject to auto-renewal. (Enrollment required)

Another Amex Blue Cash Preferred benefit is access to Amex Presale Tickets® and Amex Reserved Tickets®, which can come in handy if you’re trying to score tickets to an in-demand concert or sporting event.

The Amex Blue Cash Preferred also includes an introductory 0% APR offer that can give you some breathing room on purchases and balance transfers.

Enjoy 0% intro APR on purchases and balance transfers for 12 months from the date of account opening. After that, your APR will be a variable APR of 19.74% - 28.74%

This could be helpful if you need to transfer an existing credit card balance and have some time to pay it off before interest kicks in, or if you need some extra time to pay off a big upcoming purchase. Just make sure you pay off your balance in full before the intro period ends, or you’ll be on the hook for interest after all.

One benefit that doesn’t get enough attention is the Amex Offers program. This gives you access to targeted deals with a variety of retailers, just by registering your card and making an eligible purchase.

Different Amex cards get different offers, so having multiple Amex cards in your wallet means more chances to save. Across my Amex cards, this program saves me hundreds of dollars per year.

The Amex Blue Cash Preferred Card has fewer travel protections than more premium Amex cards, but it does come with coverage to protect your rental car when you charge the rental to this card. The coverage applies for eligible damage or theft, and it’s secondary, so it kicks in after your own car insurance. There are some exclusions, and it doesn’t include liability coverage, so check your card benefits guide for the full details.

Car rental loss and damage insurance: Eligibility and Benefit level varies by Card. Terms, Conditions, and Limitations Apply. Please visit americanexpress.com/benefitsguide for more details. Underwritten by AMEX Assurance Company. Car Rental Loss or Damage Coverage is offered through American Express Travel Related Services Company, Inc.

The Amex Blue Cash Preferred Card offers the following benefits when you shop with the card:

These are all helpful benefits that you hopefully won’t have to use (though they do provide some peace of mind). Just remember you need to purchase the relevant items with your card to get this coverage.

Purchase Protection is an embedded benefit of your Card Membership and requires no enrollment. It can help protect Covered Purchases made on your Eligible Card when they’re accidentally damaged, stolen, or lost, for up to 90 days from the Covered Purchase date. The coverage is limited to up to $10,000 per occurrence, up to $50,000 per Card Member account per calendar year. Coverage Limits Apply. Eligibility and Benefit level varies by Card. Terms, Conditions, and Limitations Apply. Please visit americanexpress.com/benefitsguide for more details. Underwritten by AMEX Assurance Company. Applies to warranties of five (5) years or less. Coverage is up to the actual amount charged to your Card for the item up to a maximum of $10,000; not to exceed $50,000 per Card Member account per calendar year. Eligibility and Benefit level varies by Card. Terms, Conditions, and Limitations Apply. Please visit americanexpress.com/benefitsguide for more details. Underwritten by AMEX Assurance Company.

I tend to lean toward points-earning cards for my own spending, but I totally get why a lot of people prefer cash back. It’s simple, flexible, and you can spend it however you want, including on travel. For that crowd, the Blue Cash Preferred is one of the best options out there.

If you’re someone who is looking to earn cash back and spends a lot at supermarkets, then the Amex Blue Cash Preferred Card is an exceptional choice, in my opinion — 6% cash back will add up quickly.

The 6% cash back on streaming, and 3% cash back on transit and gas stations, will add up quickly as well, and up to $120 Disney streaming credit could be worth more than the annual fee.

However, this isn’t the card I would be using for your everyday spending, as you’d only earn 1% cash back in the categories that aren’t eligible for bonuses. So I’d acquire this card specifically with the intent of using it in the categories offering bonuses.

So, let’s take a look at what other cards you should potentially be considering.

If you like the idea of an Amex cash back card but aren’t sure about paying an annual fee, the Blue Cash Everyday Card from American Express (learn more) is the no annual fee alternative where you can earn as high as $200 cash back after spending $2,000 in purchases on your new Card in the first six months. Welcome offers vary and you may not be eligible for an offer. Cash back is received as Reward Dollars, redeemable for statement credit or for other select options. Terms Apply.

Here’s how the two Blue Cash cards compare:

In my opinion, the Blue Cash Preferred is well worth the annual fee if you spend a meaningful amount at supermarkets. The jump from 3% to 6% on U.S. supermarkets adds up fast. If you spend $500 a month at supermarkets, that extra 3% earns you $180 more per year, which nearly doubles the annual fee.

The Blue Cash Everyday does have one unique advantage: 3% cash back on U.S. online retail purchases, which the Preferred doesn’t offer. If you do a lot of online shopping and less grocery shopping, the Everyday could actually come out ahead.

The good news is that you can always start with the Blue Cash Preferred and downgrade to the Blue Cash Everyday after the first year if the math doesn’t work out.

If you’re just looking for the simplest cash back credit card, it’s tough to go wrong with the Citi Double Cash® Card (learn more). The card offers 1% cash back when you make a purchase, and 1% cash back when you pay for that purchase, for a total of 2% back on every purchase. That’s a solid return on everyday spending, and you don’t have to keep track of any bonus spending categories.

If you’re deciding between the Blue Cash Preferred and a mid-range card from another issuer, the $95 annual fee Citi Strata Premier Card (learn more) is worth considering. These two cards serve pretty different purposes, but they’re at the same price point:

The Blue Cash Preferred wins on supermarkets and streaming, hands down. If those are your biggest spending categories, the Blue Cash Preferred is the better card.

The Strata Premier is the more well-rounded card overall. It earns 3x points across five major categories (dining, gas, supermarkets, airfare, hotels), has no foreign transaction fees, and its ThankYou points can be transferred to airline and hotel partners for potentially much more than face value. If you travel or eat out a lot, the Strata Premier likely comes out ahead.

One final card worth considering alongside the Blue Cash Preferred Card is the American Express® Gold Card (learn more). It has a significantly higher annual fee, but it’s unbeatable for earning rewards at U.S. supermarkets — you’ll earn transferable Membership Rewards points too, rather than cash back.

The Blue Cash Preferred is the best cash back card for supermarket spending, and it’s not particularly close. If groceries and streaming are a big part of your monthly budget, this card can deliver serious returns, especially with the 6% rate on both categories plus the Disney streaming credit.

The annual fee pays for itself quickly if you’re spending even a moderate amount at supermarkets. Pair it with a card like the Citi Double Cash for your non-bonused spending, and you’ve got a strong cash back setup.

Just keep in mind that the 1% rate on everyday purchases means this card works best as a specialist, not your only card. Use it where it shines, and let a flat-rate card handle the rest.

Baldini: "Bartesaghi è uno dei migliori terzini che abbia mai allenato"

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Intervistato questa mattina dai colleghi de La Gazzetta dello Sport, il CT dell'U21 Silvio Baldini ha parlato di Camarda, Cisse e Bartesaghi, ragazzi che ha avuto ed ha tutt'ora modo di allenare in Nazionale che rappresentano il futuro non solo del calcio italiano ma anche del Milan. Di seguito le sue dichiarazioni.

Terzo rossonerazzurrino è Bartesaghi: più terzino come con lei o esterno alto come nel Milan? "Davide è uno dei migliori giocatori nel ruolo che ho allenato in tanti anni, è bravo, molto professionale, maturo, introverso ma aperto e non è una contraddizione: è per dire che ha in se un potenziale enorme e me lo dimostra ogni volta che viene con noi. Fossi un allenatore qualsiasi di Serie A lo porterei con me. Per l’Under 21 Bartesaghi è un lusso. La sua spinta a sinistra per sorpassare le avversarie all’Europeo? "È un dono divino essere in questo ruolo: ragazzi e famiglia sono la cosa più cara che ho. Scegliere chi mandare in tribuna è la parte più dura. Ho detto di voler vincere Europeo e Olimpiade: lavoreremo il doppio per provarci".

Harry Kane shares update from hospital after undergoing ankle surgery

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Kane sustained a torn ligament in his left ankle in Bayern Munich’s draw with Cologne on Friday

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Harry Kane is targeting the “business end of the season” after undergoing surgery on a torn ligament in his left ankle.

Bayern Munich said the operation on the injury, sustained in Friday’s 1-1 draw with Cologne, was a success but the 33-year-old is expected to be out for months as a result.

Kane wrote on Instagram: “The highs and lows of life! Obviously very disappointed to get this injury but as always it’s an opportunity to show resilience and improvement going into 2027.

“I’ll be back fresh and ready to go for the business end of the season!

“Tough times don’t last, tough people do” – my guy @edelman11 (Julian Edelman, three-time Super Bowl winner with the New England Patriots) told me this one!

“Thank you for all the messages! Alles gut.”

Kane sustained the injury when he was scythed down in the area by Cologne defender Joel Schmied, who reached Michael Olise’s low cross first but clattered into the Ballon d’Or winner’s left leg in his follow-through at the back post.

The England captain writhed in pain and, although he gingerly got back to his feet and initially soldiered on, it was clear he could not continue and he hobbled off the field alongside two physios after treatment.

After the match Bayern confirmed Kane had torn the syndesmotic ligament and “will therefore be sidelined for the next few months”.

Kane earlier this month signed a new deal with the Bundesliga giants until June 2029 – reportedly worth in excess of £400,000 a week, making him the highest-paid player in Europe before bonuses.

Since arriving from Tottenham three years ago, Kane has bagged a scarcely believable 156 goals in 163 appearances in all competitions, helping Bayern win a league and cup double last season.

He went into the international break having a drawn a blank in his four previous matches for Bayern but he scored twice for England, including from the spot in their 3-2 Nations League win over Spain.

But after being forced off after 20 minutes at the Allianz Arena, replaced by Tom Bischof, Kane could now be facing a battle to be fully fit for England’s two-legged quarter-final against Norway in March.

Brazilian Court Orders Arrest of the Buenavista Over Corazul Dispute

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A Brazilian court ordered the arrest of Corazul’s the Buenavista at the Port of Santos after the cruise line’s exclusive Brazilian distributor claimed it was owed about $270,000 (R$1.48 million), according to a court decision dated Nov. 13.

Judge Frederico dos Santos Messias of the São Paulo State Court of Justice’s specialized maritime court granted the request from R11 Travel Viagens e Turismo against GLCL Global Cruiseline Ltd., the Cyprus-registered company that operates as Corazul.

The judge issued the order without first hearing from Corazul, the decision showed.

The order barred the Buenavista from leaving Brazilian waters. The court said the ship was anchored off Santos for repairs.

The judge set security at about $323,000 (R$1.78 million) and said the ship would be released if Corazul deposited that amount or posted a bank guarantee.

According to the decision, R11 said Corazul owed about $1.27 million (R$6.96 million) under a four-year exclusive distribution agreement signed in March. That figure included about $780,000 (EUR 675,000) in unpaid quarterly marketing contributions. After subtracting about $1 million (R$5.48 million) in passenger funds it held, R11 put the net balance at about $270,000.

The filing, as summarized by the court, described a series of disruptions including the cancellation of the Buenavista’s European season in June; the cancellation of its Oct. 23 inaugural Brazilian cruise nine days before departure and complaints about onboard service on the Oct. 30 sailing. The company has since suspended operations and R11 has stopped selling cruises on the ship.

R11 said Corazul demanded $400,000 on Nov. 4 before it would operate the Nov. 6 departure. R11 refused.

The court noted that only two of the 11 sailings scheduled between Oct. 23 and Nov. 27 operated. Corazul also suspended operations until Nov. 30 and moved the end of its Brazil season up to March 5, 2027. The judge wrote that Corazul’s own communications showed a “serious liquidity collapse.”

The dispute is headed to arbitration in São Paulo under AMCHAM, the American Chamber of Commerce for Brazil. The decision stated that R11 must file within 30 days or the order lapses. Corazul has five days after being served to contest it.

NBA team grades: Report card for all 30 teams after the first month

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The first month of the 2026-27 NBA season is in the books, and it's time to take stock of what's happened so far. Let's hand out some early grades.

It might be too soon to draw sweeping conclusions, but there's been enough basketball played to make preliminary judgements about the contenders falling short of expectations and the surprising teams surpassing what most experts anticipated. Evaluating each team's performance goes beyond its record, with roster construction, player development and overall trajectory factoring into whether a season is trending in the right direction.

Injuries to several stars and key contributors have shaped the league conversation through the opening month. Some teams have responded well to the adversity. Others have already dug themselves a hole that could prove difficult to escape. As a result, the grades cover a wide range of outcomes: six teams earned an A from USA TODAY Sports through the season's first month, while 10 received a D or F.

Here are grades for all 30 NBA teams now that the first month of the 2026-27 season is complete.

NBA PANIC METER: Which teams should worry after first month of season?

Records as of games played on Nov. 20. Preseason record projections via USA TODAY Sports.

The Hawks are right in the thick of the Eastern Conference playoff race again with guard Nickeil Alexander-Walker leading their backcourt, but there have been some troubling early signs. Jalen Johnson's production has dipped across the board from last season's breakout campaign, veteran closer C.J. McCollum is sidelined for at least another month and Atlanta has benefited from the NBA's second-easiest schedule to date. The Hawks' issues are flying under-the-radar, but they've struggled to show they belong among the East's top contenders when facing the NBA's best teams thus far.

After thriving without Jayson Tatum during much of last year's regular season, the Celtics haven't missed a beat in their first games since trading Jaylen Brown this past offseason. Tatum has elevated his playmaking, and Paul George looks rejuvenated as his new running mate in Boston. The Celtics have the NBA's best NET rating as of Nov. 18 and even got the better of Brown in their first matchup against the Philadelphia 76ers.

The Nets' roster is a mess because they've used seven first-round draft picks the past two years and none has demonstrated franchise-player potential to this point. Their top three guards (Egor Demin, Nolan Traore and Mikel Brown Jr.) haven't played much together yet due to minutes restrictions and skillset overlap. Their past four losses have come by an average of 24.5 points, and Julius Randle was spotted yelling at his teammates on the bench before calling their Nov. 18 loss to the Chicago Bulls "one of the worst games I've been a part of." After years of stockpiling assets, the Nets' rebuild still doesn't appear to have a clear path forward.

The Hornets perhaps expected to take a step back after trading LaMelo Ball this past offseason, but their start has muted much of the momentum generated from last year's encouraging run to the playoffs. Kon Knueppel lasted just nine minutes in his season debut before suffering a hamstring injury, and he's one of several key players to have missed games due to injury. The team, meanwhile, has the worst offensive rating in the league after finishing with the NBA's fifth-most efficient offense last season. Brandon Miller is shooting less than 35% from the field as the top offensive option, and Naz Reid, the primary acquisition in the Ball deal, was benched four games into the season by Hornets coach Charles Lee for "not meeting Hornets' standards." Just one month into the season, nearly everything that could have gone wrong for Charlotte has.

The Bulls are starting to emerge from a 1-9 start to the season, though their recent surge is happening right as No. 3 overall pick Caleb Wilson was diagnosed with a broken hand that will keep him out for the next few months. Wilson showed intriguing flashes in his early appearances, but it's still unclear whether Chicago's core featuring Josh Giddey and Matas Buzelis is viable long-term. The Bulls feel closer to escaping the bottom-half of the Eastern Conference than they have been because of Wilson, but his injury means they'll have to wait longer to figure out exactly what they have.

The Cavaliers are off to a rougher-than-expected start to the season, and it's particularly concerning because their starters are the reason they're scuffling along. James Harden is showing his age more regularly, struggling to finish at the rim and get to the free throw line. Evan Mobley has already suffered a toe injury. Peyton Watson, the team's main offseason acquisition, looked completely out of sync before missing action recently with a back injury. Rookie Meleek Thomas and forward Mario Hezonja have been bright spots off the bench, but Cleveland's core has failed to resemble the group that reached the Eastern Conference finals last season.

Cooper Flagg appears to have taken another step forward in his development after winning Rookie of the Year, and his fit alongside Kyrie Irving has been better than many expected under new coach Dusty May. They've positioned the Mavericks as a legitimate play-in contender despite playing one of the tougher schedules in the league through 15 games. Their early success together strengthens the case for Irving to remain in Dallas beyond the NBA trade deadline. With a first-year NBA coach and a 19-year-old franchise star, the Mavericks are outperforming expectations through the season's first month.

The Nuggets remain a threat in the Western Conference playoff race, but there are some early signs that Denver's reign as a legitimate title contender could be waning. Nikola Jokic's 3-point shot has largely disappeared from his repertoire, a development that began after he returned from a wrist injury last season. His points, rebounds and assists per game have decreased compared to recent years. Jamal Murray's strong play has been offset by relatively uninspiring starts for veterans Aaron Gordon and DeMar DeRozan. It's no coincidence the team has gone from first in the league in offensive rating last year to 10th as of Nov. 19. If Jokic can't return to his MVP form and cover some of the roster flaws like he so often has over the past five years, it's hard to envision this group being a championship contender.

The Pistons were a trendy regression candidate in the East, but they've already got wins over the Boston Celtics, Miami Heat, Philadelphia 76ers and San Antonio Spurs through the first month of the season. Cade Cunningham is back in the MVP conversation, and Ausar Thompson is becoming one of the league's most disruptive two-way playmakers. Early injuries to Jalen Duren and offseason addition Isaiah Joe have taken a little shine off Detroit's strong start, but guard Daniss Jenkins is beginning to blossom into the secondary perimeter scoring option Cunningham has lacked in previous seasons.

The Warriors got an unexpected gift when rookie Yaxel Lendeborg quickly emerged as the leading NBA Rookie of the Year candidate and helped solve the team's void on the wing with Jimmy Butler sidelined. They reeled off eight-straight wins with Lendeborg serving as Steph Curry's sidekick. But Golden State dropped five games in a row after Curry suffered an oblique injury on Nov. 11, a development that has exposed Draymond Green's limitations and the lack of offense creation on the roster beyond Curry. Even so, Curry's eventual return and the prospect of Butler coming back gives Golden State more intrigue and upside than most expected entering the season.

The Rockets have looked like NBA title contenders through the season's first month, with the return of point guard Fred Van Vleet helping jump start the league's second-most efficient offense. Houston's turnovers are way down and its 3-point shooting is among the best in the NBA at the moment. Alperen Sengun, meanwhile, is on pace to break his career highs in points and assists as part of the Rockets' deep front court, and Kevin Durant remains one of the NBA's elite scorers. This appears to be one of the NBA's most complete rosters, blending ascending young talent with veterans who have extensive playoff experience. Considering the Rockets also possess the assets to make another significant move before the trade deadline, they may be San Antonio's biggest threat in the Western Conference.

The Pacers were hoping to rebound from last season's injury-induced tank, but they lost star Tyrese Haliburton to a hamstring injury after just four games. Indiana is one of the worst shooting teams in the league after one month, and it's wasting another strong start from Pascal Siakam. The lack of shooting has complicated Ivica Zubac's transition to the lineup, with poor spacing creating fit issues for a player Indiana spent considerable assets to acquire. He's on pace for his worst statistical season in six years. The Pacers entered the season expecting to rejoin the Eastern Conference contender tier, but they look much closer to a lottery team than a playoff threat right now.

The Clippers are in rebuilding mode and off to an uneven start, with the expectation they could be sellers at the trade deadline. The good news is that Darius Garland, the centerpiece of last year's James Harden trade, appears to have rediscovered his All-Star form after an injury-riddled 2025-26 season. The development of first-round pick Keaton Wagler alongside Garland is likely more important than the results the rest of the way, and he's shooting better than 40% from 3-point range since Nov. 2. The Clippers are one of the league's better shooting teams overall, but they've offset that strength by ranking among the NBA's most turnover-prone teams. They're likely headed for the lottery, but that's exactly where most anticipated them to be.

The Lakers are off to a fantastic start with Luka Dončić a leading MVP candidate and on pace to surpass Michael Jordan for the highest scoring average (38.1) since the 1976 NBA/ABA merger. Walker Kessler is also providing the dominating interior presence Los Angeles hoped for when it traded for him this offseason and gave him a huge contract. Kessler is averaging a double-double, while Austin Reaves is scoring more efficiently than at any point in his career. If Dončić continues to play at this level, the Lakers will be in the NBA title discussion come playoff time.

NBA MVP RANKINGS: There's a new No. 1, as Luka Dončić cedes ground

The Grizzlies have been the biggest surprise of the 2026-27 season thus far, vaulting toward the top of the Western Conference standings when many expected them to begin a rebuild after trading away Ja Morant, Jaren Jackson Jr. and Desmond Bane. Instead, the team's recent draft picks, particularly Cameron Boozer and Cedric Coward, have helped spearhead a balanced and remarkably deep roster that already looks like a playoff contender. A recent injury to veteran Ty Jerome looms large, but Cam Spencer has filled the void admirably thus far. Memphis owns the NBA's best offensive rating as of Nov. 20. Few saw that coming.

NBA observers were in panic mode when the Heat began the Giannis Antetokounmpo era with four losses in a row, but Miami is 9-2 since then with an increasingly potent offense built around pace and space. Antetokounmpo is leading the Heat with the fourth-highest scoring average in the NBA (28.2 points), and Davion Mitchell leads the NBA in assists per game. Only the Spurs' starters have a better NET rating than the Heat lineup featuring Antetokounmpo, Bam Adebayo, Klay Thompson, Mitchell and Pelle Larsson among groups that have played at least 65 minutes together. One month into the season, Miami appears to have already discovered the lineup it can trust most when playoff games are on the line.

The Bucks were expected to be bad this season after trading Giannis Antetokounmpo, but they're off to the worst start in franchise history with the league's worst record and own the worst NET rating in the Eastern Conference as of Nov. 20. With veterans like Myles Turner, Kyle Kuzma and Gary Trent Jr. taking up salary cap space after being acquired in prior seasons, this rebuild appears to only just be beginning. Milwaukee entered the post-Giannis era without a clear franchise cornerstone, and the first month of the season has done little to change that. It will be painful for Bucks' fans this season.

The Timberwolves have done a fine job keeping pace in the competitive Western Conference after losing Anthony Edwards to a knee injury that required arthoscopic surgery. LaMelo Ball, Ayo Dosunmu, Jaden McDaniels and Rudy Gobert have formed a well-rounded core that could be dangerous if Edwards returns before the playoffs. There is no timetable on his recovery, though Edwards told reporters on Nov. 19 he is planning to to come back this season. Minnesota's ceiling ultimately depends on his health.

The Pelicans got off to a bad start, won four games in a row and now appear headed nowhere fast once again. Dejounte Murray's recent trade request brought renewed attention to one of the NBA's most confusing roster situations. New Orleans hasn't been able to win consistently despite Zion Williamson enjoying one of the healthiest stretches of his career, and Trey Murphy III is increasingly viewed as one of the league's more attractive trade targets. Jeremiah Fears and Derik Queen are intriguing young players, but both have contributed to the defensive issues that have left New Orleans among the NBA's worst teams on that end of the floor. Murray, meanwhile, is owed enough money that moving him won't be simple. If this start continues, Joe Dumars would have to consider a roster overhaul.

The Knicks deserve the benefit of the doubt after a sluggish start coming off an NBA championship, though the early-season struggles of Josh Hart and Mikal Bridges, as well as the ongoing contract situation involving Karl-Anthony Towns, bear watching. Jalen Brunson and Towns continue to form one of the NBA's most potent duos, and New York remains firmly positioned in the Eastern Conference playoff picture thanks in large part to one of the league's best defenses. The Knicks proved last season they can reach another level when the games matter most.

The Thunder are doing their best to stay afloat in the crowded Western Conference standings playing without Jalen Williams, Chet Holmgren and Cason Wallace due to injuries. Shai Gilgeous-Alexander remains one of the league's best players, and OKC's deep reservoir of first-round picks is paying off with younger players like Ajay Mitchell and rookie Aday Mara successfully stepping into larger roles. The Thunder have survived a difficult opening month and came out looking capable of remaining in the playoff mix. But bad injury luck could prevent them from contending for a title this season.

The Magic hoped to emerge as Eastern Conference contenders, but they finished the opening month of the season below .500 and lost star Paolo Banchero to injury after just two games. A recent four-game winning streak that included a victory over the Boston Celtics showcased the potential of a group built around Franz Wagner, Desmond Bane, Anthony Black and a healthy Jalen Suggs. But the franchise is still no closer to determining whether Banchero and Wagner can thrive together as a long-term foundation. Until Banchero returns, that question will remain unanswered, and Orlando appears destined to be part of the crowded pack of Eastern Conference teams battling to avoid the play-in tournament.

After considerable preseason hype and past stumbles out of the gate for LeBron James-led superteams, the Sixers finished the first month of the 2026-27 season tied with the Spurs for the NBA's best record. James and Jaylen Brown have blended in well with Tyrese Maxey, Joel Embiid and VJ Edgecombe, though the team's starting lineup had a surprising -4.9 NET rating playing together through 16 games. It speaks to some of the unexpected depth Philadelphia enjoys, with Dean Wade, Anfernee Simons and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope forming a versatile rotation. One month into the season, the 76ers look like the team to beat in the Eastern Conference.

The Suns have rebounded from a rough start to the season despite playing one of the toughest schedules in the league, and Devin Booker remains among the NBA's top 12 scorers. But their two most-used lineups have been outscored by an average net rating of 15.5 points per 100 possessions. Jalen Green is having the least productive season of his career, and centers Oso Ighodaro and Khaman Maluach have looked overmatched while replacing the injured Mark Williams. As currently constructed, Phoenix looks capable of reaching the play-in tournament. But unless the Suns can find an upgrade before the trade deadline, their ceiling appears to be a first-round playoff exit.

The Trail Blazers have taken a step forward with wins over the Lakers and Thunder already. Damian Lillard is providing a perimeter scoring punch, and his presence hasn't come at the expense of Deni Avdija. It's turned a lackluster offense into one with a top-10 NET rating thus far. It remains to be seen how Ja Morant's return from a hamstring injury affects the rotation, but early returns suggest Portland's crowded backcourt can co-exist. This might not be a legitimate title contender, but the Trail Blazers look as feisty as they have at any point since Lillard led them to the Western Conference Finals in 2019.

The Kings appear to be the most dysfunctional team in the league, with a core of expensive veteran players who don't seem interested in remaining in Sacramento but lack significant trade value. Rookie Darius Acuff Jr. has been a bright spot, on pace to average the most points by a first-year player since Michael Jordan. But the Kings haven't translated that production into wins and they have the league's worst NET rating. Meanwhile, last year's breakout rookie, 23-year-old center Maxime Raynaud, has yet to play due to injury. This directionless franchise is no closer to finding its way.

The Spurs have looked every bit like a team on a mission to avenge last June's NBA Finals loss, highlighted by a recent 12-game winning streak. Victor Wembanyama is an MVP candidate once again, and the returning core of Dylan Harper, Stephon Castle and De'Aaron Fox has made the absence of free-agent acquisition Tobias Harris largely an afterthought. A recent hip injury to Harper does not appear to be serious, and San Antonio is deep enough to withstand his absence if he misses additional time. One month into the season, the Spurs look like the favorites to win the NBA title.

After emerging as one of the NBA's pleasant surprises last season, the Raptors have stumbled out of the gate this year. Kawhi Leonard has yet to play because of a knee injury, and Scottie Barnes has also missed time. The team's defense has gone from a strength to a weakness, particularly in close games, and Toronto ranks among the NBA's worst rebounding teams because of a wing-heavy roster that lacks a reliable center. The Raptors traded for Leonard with championship aspirations in mind, but the first month of that pursuit has been anything but encouraging.

The Jazz's hopes of emerging as a playoff contender after a lengthy rebuilding have been decimated by injuries to Lauri Markannen and Jaren Jackson Jr., as well as a disastrous start that featured a 12-game losing streak. Keyonte George played more like a rising star in two recent Utah wins and, along with rookie Darryn Peterson and 20-year-old Ace Bailey, gives the franchise a promising young core. But the Jazz expected to compete for a postseason berth and that might not be possible after an opening month that could hardly have gone worse.

The Wizards remain near the bottom of the Eastern Conference standings, and their decision to give Trae Young a $212 million contract extension is already drawing scrutiny. Young is shooting a career-worst 30.4% from 3-point range, and Washington ranks as the NBA's second-worst 3-point shooting team. Opponents have also targeted Young defensively in crunch time. The team's struggles are particularly concerning because Anthony Davis is healthy and playing well, while rookie AJ Dybantsa has shown encouraging flashes despite struggling to shoot from outside since returning from injury. Washington may still have enough talent to reach the playoffs in the East, but its biggest investment is underperforming.

What's next for Arch Manning's future as Texas QB partakes in Senior Day?

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

"Closing Time" by Semisonic may soon be playing in the background for Arch Manning, as the possibility of his college football career coming to an end is looming, though that outcome is far from certain.

One milestone, however, is guaranteed for the Texas quarterback in Week 12: Senior Day.

Manning is set to take part in the senior day festivities ahead of Texas' Week 12 matchup against Arkansas at DKR-Texas Memorial Stadium on Saturday, joining the rest of the Longhorns' senior class as they are recognized for their contributions to the program during their time on the Forty Acres.

College football Fraud Alert: Texas wins, but Arch Manning is guilty

Manning has endured another up-and-down season for the Longhorns with the highs including a come-from-behind win against then-No. 1 Ohio State and wins against Tennessee and Ole Miss, while lows include turning the ball over multiple times in the fourth quarter in back-to-back losses to Oklahoma and Florida.

Despite all that, Manning still holds a strong 18-5 record while serving as Texas' full-time starter, which includes a 9-2 mark against top-ranked opponents.

That all said, there is a possibility of Manning prolonging his career. Once viewed as a near lock for the No. 1 overall pick in the NFL draft, Manning's draft stock is no longer as clear-cut as it once appeared, and he has another year of eligibility remaining, so a return to college football is far from an unrealistic scenario.

Here's what to know about his future plans:

Yes, Manning will partake in Texas' senior day festivities on Saturday ahead of its final home game of the season against Arkansas.

"I'm a senior, so I'm doing it," Manning said earlier this week.

Manning's decision to return to the Longhorns for the 2026 season followed in line with what each of his uncles, Eli and Peyton Manning, and his grandfather, Archie Manning, did by exhausting their eligibility before moving on to play in the NFL. Peyton and Eli Manning were both the No. 1 picks of their respective drafts in 1998 and 2004, while Archie Manning was the No. 2 overall pick in the 1971 NFL Draft.

While Manning is a senior and will be part of senior day on Saturday, it doesn't necessarily mean he'll be moving on from college or the Longhorns after this season. How come? Well, Manning has an extra year of eligibility — as he redshirted his true freshman season after appearing in just three games — so he could return to Texas for another season or transfer somewhere else. Or he could declare for the NFL draft.

Trick Play: Texas pulls off slick trick play against Lane Kiffin, using Arch Manning as decoy

Yes, Manning is eligible for the 2027 NFL Draft, which is the second straight draft he's eligible to declare.

It remains unclear whether Manning will declare after the Longhorns' season for the NFL draft or return for a fifth season. The Longhorns have KJ Lacey Jr. and Dia Bell already on their roster as Manning's backups, and have three-star quarterback Ty Knuster verbally committed to their 2027 recruiting class.

According to NFL Operations, to be eligible for the draft, a player must have been removed from high school for three years to be eligible.

"To be eligible for the draft, players must have been out of high school for at least three years and must have used up their college eligibility before the start of the next college football season. Underclassmen and players who have graduated before using all their college eligibility may request the league's approval to enter the draft early.

"Players are draft-eligible only in the year after the end of their college eligibility."

Manning is listed as the No. 22-ranked quarterback by ESPN's Mel Kiper Jr. in the 2026 NFL Draft class.

Polling NFL mock drafts from several major outlets — such as USA TODAY, CBS Sports and Sports Illustrated The 6-foot-4 quarterback is not in any first-round projections as of right now.

Back in late October, in the midst of a stretch of back-to-back losses to Oklahoma and Florida, an NFL scout told USA TODAY Sports' Matt Hayes that Manning's draft stock hasn't exactly been elevated by his then-19 games as a full-time starter at Texas despite having some notable top-ranked wins on his resume.

"Someone might take him in the third or fourth round right now. But a 1? No chance," the NFL scout told USA TODAY Sports. "There's any number of issues. Decision-making and arm strength at the top of the list."

Napoli & Juventus weigh up a January swoop for Joshua Zirkzee

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Both Napoli and Juventus are closely monitoring Manchester United’s Joshua Zirkzee ahead of the January transfer window, according to Italian transfer guru Fabrizio Romano.

While Zirkzee might have been eventually stayed put at Old Trafford over the summer following heavy speculation over a move away, the Dutch international failed to find any real regular opportunities under now-former United boss Michael Carrick.

Despite bagging a maiden Premier League strike of the season during his seven-minute cameo against Aston Villa prior to November’s international break, Zirkzee has yet to make a first start across any competition.

While Slovenian marksman Benjamin Sesko might have been ruled out for the remainder of 2026 due to an extended shin injury, the 25-year-old has failed to establish himself as a worthy deputy.

Nevertheless, despite registering just six goals in his 62 Premier League appearances since arriving at Old Trafford back in the summer of 2024, Romano is reporting that two Italian icons are planning on offering Zirkzee as Manchester United exit in January.

With the one-time Bayern Munich youngster reportedly making it clear that he is seeking regular first-team opportunities, both Napoli and Juventus are keeping a close eye on proceedings.

The Italian transfer expert suggested this week that alongside Napoli holding strong links with Zirkzee’s representatives, Juventus could be keen on a potential deal should it suitable for all parties.

10 Crime Shows With the Greatest Villain Performances, Ranked

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

One of the greatest strengths of crime television is its ability to skew moral compasses and deliver fascinating, complex characters that challenge our understanding of humanity. This is true of detective drama and organized crime suspense, and it is particularly true of the many great villains that populate these series, whether they are psychotic serial killers defined by their sinister presence or oddly charismatic crooks who are as charming as they are chilling.

It goes without saying that the performances of such characters are instrumental in determining their quality. Everything from the subtleties of facial expressions to the rhythm of line delivery, and to the physicality they are presented with, is all key choices actors can make in defining the brand of villainy their character exudes. Whether viewers hold a wicked love for them or dread every second of screen time they receive, these crime series find astonishing highlights in the form of their biggest and baddest antagonists.

Despite only appearing in the third season of Boardwalk Empire, Gyp Rosetti (Bobby Cannavale) stands as one of the Prohibition-era gangster series’ most piercing and unforgettable characters. Within a story that often feels methodical and pragmatic, Rosetti injects a tense volatility into every single scene he appears in, which is a testament to the astonishing writing of the character and Cannavale’s note-perfect performance of such an explosive character.

Whereas so many of the other adversaries Nucky Thompson (Steve Buscemi) encounters attempt to outsmart him through business maneuvers or political plotting, Rosetti relies on ferocious, bloodthirsty brutality and unwavering intent. His destructive, overwhelming personality is only made all the more commanding by the character’s sensitivity to perceived slights, especially as Cannavale imbues every second of screen time he gets with the simmering suspense of a ticking time bomb. Whether he’s erupting into a fit of rage or hurling oddly elegant insults, Cannavale makes Rosetti one of the most intimidating and unpleasant TV antagonists of all time.

As a grim descent into the criminal underbelly of Gotham City, The Penguin marks one of HBO’s more compelling productions of the decade so far, a rich mixture of comic-book style and gangland brutality. Colin Farrell’s lead performance is a driving force of this, but the miniseries benefits from its vast array of striking characters, with Cristin Milioti’s turn as criminal heiress Sofia Falcone being the noteworthy standout as she blends an erratic, unpredictable ruthlessness with underlying vulnerability and sympathetic sorrow.

Milioti worked closely with both movement coach Julia Crockett and the production’s costuming department to perfect Sofia’s physicality. Through every movement, she embodies the volatility of her explosive aspirations fighting to break free from her past of repression and institutionalization. In isolation, she is exceptional — the fourth episode’s focus on her time in Arkham Asylum was instrumental in her Emmy Award win — but the crackling, high-octane tension she brings to scenes with Farrell is her true triumph, crafting a complex yet purposeful obstacle to Cobb’s own ambitions of controlling organized crime in Gotham.

Purely as a superhero villain, Wilson Fisk (Vincent D’Onofrio), otherwise known as Kingpin, is a perfect foil to Daredevil (Charlie Cox), with his brutish strength challenging the hero’s more precise and calculating fighting style. However, it isn’t the imposing physicality that makes Fisk such a great antagonist, but rather the depth, nuance, and tragedy D’Onofrio realizes in the character in addition to his intimidating frame.

He plays the part with a certain childlike vulnerability, a stiff social awkwardness that is almost endearing in tandem with Fisk’s sophistication and his love for Vanessa (Ayelet Zurer). His stammering, soft-spoken nature only makes his volatile rage all the more terrifying, grounding the character in his insecurities that can give way to a destructive wrath at any moment. D’Onofrio’s ability to navigate such a polarizing dichotomy is astonishing, bringing both the physical gravitas and the timid personality together to make for one of the best mob boss bad guys in the medium’s history.

It may be difficult to separate hero from villain in a series like The Sopranos. Everyone is bad. But some are worse than others, as Tony Soprano’s (James Gandolfini) rogues' gallery perfectly illustrates. Amid a collection of menacing mobsters, Joe Pantoliano’s Ralphie Cifaretto is a striking standout, with the duality Pantoliano brings to the role being a significant reason for this.

The character is both charming and reprehensible. He has an air of charisma and quick-witted comedy that creates some of the more memorable and wickedly hilarious scenes in the series, but his volatile nature also underlines some of the more disturbing moments as well. His abrupt, sadistic murder of Tracee (Ariel Kiley) is a frightfully disgusting scene that tends to linger in the minds of viewers, one only made more chilling by the way Pantoliano pivots straight back to Ralphie’s more cheerful demeanor almost immediately after. Even within Tony’s crew, he is a disconcerting, unpredictable presence. That he made such a visceral impression despite featuring in just 21 of The Sopranos’ 86 episodes is a testament to Pantoliano’s performance.

At its best, Killing Eve presents a mixture of high-octane spy thrills and international crime drama that is nothing short of addictive. Its two lead actresses are instrumental in creating this gripping atmosphere of anxiety and awe, with Jodie Comer utterly outstanding as the brutal yet brilliant international assassin, Villanelle. While the character is initially presented as a ferocious, psychotic killer, she gradually evolves as her complex cat-and-mouse obsession with MI5 agent Eve Polastri (Sandra Oh) grows increasingly intense and intimate.

Comer is brilliant at exploring the depths of Villanelle while still presenting her as a ruthless, unpredictable villain. Her ability to switch between accents, dialects, and even languages as Villanelle navigates the world of international crime is captivating, making for a frightfully capable antagonist who exudes a sense of magnetism with her mixture of callous practicality and sadistic ire. Even slight facial twitches become transfixing, whether she’s conveying boredom, curiosity, or menace. The end result is a bold villain laced with theatricality and intrigue who is able to be profoundly complex and absolutely sinister at once.

Four criminal masterminds. One of them was always going to be yours. Here is who comes for you, why they picked you out of everyone available, and what they want from the encounter.

Your nemesis does not want to beat you. He wants to know you completely, and he is patient enough to wait for it. The frightening part is how good the conversation is.

Your nemesis is not after money, territory or revenge. He is after a demonstration, and you are the person he has chosen to prove his point on.

Your nemesis respects you, which sounds flattering until you work out what it means. He chose you because nobody else was worth his time.

Your nemesis has no personal interest in you whatsoever, and that is precisely what makes him unsurvivable. You are not a rival. You are a stop on the way.

Justified becomes more than just a standard police procedural series off the back of its quirks and eccentricities. The setting, the style, and the tone all play a pivotal part in this, as do the characters themselves, with few being as defining as the overarching antagonist, Boyd Crowder (Walton Goggins). Crowder was originally set to die in the pilot episode, but Goggins’ infectious screen presence saw the writers extend his arc across the six-season stretch, with Boyd going from being a white supremacist to a born-again preacher with a penchant for organized crime.

The theatricality Goggins imbues in the part is transfixing. Lines aren’t simply recited; they are danced with, performed in the truest sense of the word. What could so easily have been a typical redneck villain is instead a captivating cacophony of Southern charm, intellectual curiosity, and treacherous, underlying vulnerability. He is the epitome of a villain you can’t help but love, a grandiose persona of mayhem whose chemistry with Timothy Olyphant’s U.S. Marshal Raylan Givens is an essential pillar of Justified’s arresting allure.

Sometimes, in great detective drama, the only thing more satisfying than seeing the hero solve the case is seeing them meet their match. Sherlock operates on this idea magnificently. Introduced in the Season 1 finale, Andrew Scott’s Jim Moriarty is the perfect foil to Sherlock’s (Benedict Cumberbatch) analytical expertise, and one that perfectly fits into the series’ modernizing take on Arthur Conan Doyle’s classic sleuth stories.

Instead of moustache-twirling vaudevillian villainy, Moriarty is a cold and calculating criminal mastermind who pushes Holmes to his limit and beyond. The theatricality and energized fun that Scott brings to the role is a huge factor as to why the character is so unforgettable, but his ruthless cunning is what establishes him in the viewer’s mind in the first place. Both within the context of the story—being the perfect adversary to Sherlock—and on his own merits—his mesmerizing mixture of twisted humor, arresting charisma, and genuine menace—Jim Moriarty is a true icon of television antagonism.

There are few who would dispute the notion that Dexter’s fourth season is its strongest. It benefits from unrelenting high-stakes tension, a number of devastating plot twists, and the impeccable performance of John Lithgow as the overarching antagonist, Arthur Mitchell. Also known as The Trinity Killer, Mitchell presents a chilling duality between suburban normality and murderous rage, and it’s a divide that Lithgow excels at exploring and blurring as his cat-and-mouse game with Dexter (Michael C. Hall) escalates throughout the season.

However, Mitchell isn’t presented merely as being a sadistic serial killer hiding in the cozy comfort of suburbia, but is rather dissected as a fascinating, often tragic man governed by an overbearing God-complex, festering trauma, and a deeply disturbed inner child. It is undeniably terrifying, but there is an acute pathos that viewers are compelled to feel for him as well. Complimented by his seismic impact on Dexter’s personal life, Arthur Mitchell is both a devastating villain and a bitterly complex soul, with Lithgow’s deft portrayal of him making for one of the best antagonists in crime television.

It is impossible to overstate the achievement of what Mads Mikkelsen achieved with his performance throughout Hannibal. Not only did he have to epitomize true, calculating evil in a series loaded with frightful antagonists, but he also had to inherit the role from Anthony Hopkins’ iconic, Academy Award-winning turn in The Silence of the Lambs. He excels with that same sense of refined, sophisticated menace, but he also finds room to impart his own nuances to the character.

Through his manipulative working relationship with traumatized FBI criminal profiler Will Graham (Hugh Dancy), Lecter connives to garner control while still satiating his own homicidal impulses. Mikkelsen approaches the character as a fallen angel, a devilish villain alien to human morality cloaked in a veneer of sharp professionalism. The only glimpses behind the façade, particularly early in the series, are subtle facial twitches and shifts in posture. It’s eerily graceful, completely engaging, and one of the best television performances of the 2010s.

Breaking Bad has no shortage of great villains, from the savagely unpredictable Tuco (Raymond Cruz) to the sadistic Todd (Jesse Plemons), but it is impossible to look beyond Giancarlo Esposito’s Gustavo Fring as being the series’ greatest antagonist. In contrast to the show’s many explosive and eccentric antagonists, Fring is an image of cold poise, a terrifying entity of restraint and discipline harboring a cunning, murderous mind beneath the surface of a fast-food restaurant chain owner.

With Esposito’s tight and tense performance capable of injecting both comedy and chilling suspense into scenes, Fring’s lack of theatricality becomes a spectacle in itself. This quiet nature is imbued with tremendous power courtesy of Esposito’s rhythmic yet calm line delivery and his icily still presence, both of which present him as a figure of ruthless efficiency. Whether Walter White (Bryan Cranston) is working under Fring or waging a deadly war of wits against him, the cartel drug lord exudes a frightful yet transfixing air of lethal composure that makes him the greatest crime TV villain of all time as well as one of the best characters the medium has ever seen.

Why Serie A players are wearing red paint on their faces in Week 12 fixtures

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Serie A players and coaches will be donning a splash red paint across their cheeks during the 12th round of league fixtures coming up this weekend to raise awareness for the ‘Un Rosso alla Violenza (‘a red card to violence’) campaign.

This will be the 10th time that Serie A has participated in the Rosso alla Violenza campaign, which is aimed to raise awareness around domestic violence towards women.

A number of gestures to raise awareness for the campaign are planned for the 12th round of Serie A fixtures: Players, coaches and officials will don the red face paint, and for the first time, supporters in the stands have been encouraged to participate as well.

Serie A captains will also wear a symbolic armband during Week 12. The hashtag #unrossoallaviolenza and several adverts and graphics will be shown in the stadiums and will be broadcast on TV ahead of kick-off.

“Serie A has been at the forefront of the fight against violence towards women for 10 years, and coincide with the international day for the elimination of violence against women during the 12th round of league fixtures, we will renew our determination and commitment to combating all forms of abuse and especially towards women,” said Lega Serie A President Ezio Simonelli.

Marco Chiesara, President of WeWorld, who organise the campaign, added: “Violence against women is not a private matter and does not only concern those who suffer from it. It is a collective responsibility. With this campaign, we want to reach a wide audience, particularly men, and invite them to question their own role in combating it.”

Peter Young is a Senior Reporter with Football Italia.

Bayern Munich considering Jamal Musiala sale after health problems

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Health  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Musiala was diagnosed with a “neurological dysfunction” after suffering an on-pitch collapse twice in a matter of days during pre-season

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Bayern Munich are considering selling Jamal Musiala over concerns that his ongoing health problems will prevent him from returning to his best form.

Musiala, 23, was diagnosed with a “neurological dysfunction” after suffering an on-pitch collapse twice in a matter of days during pre-season friendlies against Heidenheim and RB Leipzig.

The Germany international said he was “very optimistic... there is no additional health risk beyond this” and indicated the condition would not hamper his highly promising career.

However, Bayern chief Karl-Heinz Rummenigge has now admitted that the club are assessing his future amid a downturn in performances, publicly questioning whether the club can now justify his lofty salary.

Rummenigge told Sky Germany: “He has a health-related issue. It's also well known that he has a contract involving very substantial sums of money. As a club, we have to consider how to deal with that situation. I think that's responsible and perfectly normal.

“What we know unfortunately suggests it will be difficult for him to get back to the level he has often shown here in this stadium.”

Musiala was reportedly Bayern’s best-paid player until Ballon d’Or winner Harry Kane penned a contract extension earlier this month.

He has been limited to just four appearances in the Bundesliga this term due to a muscle injury, scoring one goal and notching one assist, with fitness problems continuing to plague him after spending over half of last season out with a broken leg.

Rummenigge says “a decision will have to be made” over his Musiala if he fails to show signs of improvement, but admitted that the former Chelsea academy product’s health issues would make a sale difficult.

Rummenigge added: “We need to talk to the family, the player, and the agency, because then these sums of money, sorry, with all due respect, are no longer feasible.

“I cannot imagine that Jamal wants to leave the club or that any other club would buy him under these conditions.

“We are all interested in his return, ideally in top form. But what happens if that isn’t the case? Then we still have to think about the finances.

“The discussion hasn’t taken place yet. These are just ideas at this stage.”

Fortnite Officially Disables Geysers Due To Flying Exploit

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Fortnite players are coming up with some incredibly inventive ways to make the Pirate Shop Access grind a little easier. Endlessly opening chests gets tedious, fast, and the community wants to scratch off as many lottery tickets as it can.

One pretty busted way of doing that was ramming an SUV into a geyser and jumping out to infinitely glide, essentially giving players flight. That might not sound useful, but you could fly into and destroy entire buildings, opening hundreds of chests in minutes.

Epic Games quickly caught wind of what players were doing and, earlier this morning, disabled geysers. Considering the next Treasure Hours is today, with boosted token odds, it's no wonder the team worked so quickly to shut this one down.

This comes just days after the Respawn Rumble exploit. For whatever reason, rewards — including the Pirate Shop Access tokens — were enabled, and it was clear this was unintentional because they popped up with greyed-out chest icons and the name, 'PH' (placeholder).

Since you would respawn infinitely in Respawn Rumble, players took advantage of this open window to rush the storm and open however many chests they could. Later that day, Epic rolled out a fix.

Don't get the RNG twisted, though. Opening 137,500 chests doesn't guarantee you a token. There's a 0.00073% chance of finding the Pirate Shop Access, and the odds reset on every single chest. The reason players are finding inventive ways to open as many chests as possible is simply to roll the dice more, but your odds are in the toilet no matter how many you open.

Still, as far as Pirate Shop Access grinds go, flying like Superman into every building, boat, and pirate cove is one of the more creative.

Fortnite players are already getting fed up hunting for Pirate Shop tokens, and are calling on Epic Games to disclose the odds.

If you haven't given up and moved onto GTA 6 yet, the next Treasure Hours window is later today at 11am PT (7pm GMT), with 1.5x boosted odds (meaning you have a 1 in 91,667 chance of finding a token, or a 0.00109% chance). You won't be able to open 500 chests in minutes with the SUV-geyser trick, but if you come back at 6pm PT (2am GMT), the odds will be doubled.

Fortnite has finally pulled back the curtain and revealed the Pirate Shop Access drop rates.

L'ex Jungdal: “Allenarsi con Maignan e Donnarumma è stata la miglior scuola possibile. Tresoldi? Un grande. Prendetelo!”

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Quanti possono dire di essersi allenati al fianco di Gianluigi Donnarumma e Mike Maignan quando erano ancora giovanissimi? Andreas Jungdal è uno di questi. Arrivato al Milan nel 2019 quando aveva appena 17 anni, il portiere danese ha avuto il privilegio di condividere il campo con due dei migliori estremi difensori della sua generazione, imparando anche da una leggenda rossonera come Nelson Dida. Oggi, dopo aver intrapreso il proprio percorso lontano da Milano, continua a seguire con affetto il club che tifava da bambino. In esclusiva ai microfoni di MilanNews, nell’intervista realizzata dal giornalista Alessandro Schiavone, l'attuale portiere titolare del K.V.C Westerlo in Belgio e riserva della nazionale danese ripercorre gli anni in rossonero, mette a confronto Donnarumma e Maignan, racconta il rapporto con Dida e si sofferma anche su Nicolò Tresoldi, l’attaccante italo-tedesco che sta brillando in Belgio e che consiglierebbe senza esitazioni al Milan. Protagonista di una grande serata, con un rigore parato. Hai imparato da Gigio come si parano i rigori? “Sì, si può dire così (ride, ndr). Vabbè, ho imparato tanto da Gigio e pure da Mike. Insomma, da tutti i portieri del Milan! Allenarmi con loro due è stata una vera scuola per portieri.”   Hai imparato di più da Maignan o da Donnarumma?  “Vabbè, sono due portieri molto diversi. Secondo me Gigio sulla linea è un portiere incredibile, mentre Mike magari con la palla è più forte. Hanno qualità diverse e sono stato fortunato a imparare da tutti e due.”   Ti sei allenato due anni con Donnarumma, dal 2019 al 2021, e 18 mesi con Mike Maignan, dall'estate 2021 al gennaio 2023. Puoi dirci chi è più forte?  “Mah, io non posso dire chi è più forte (ride, ndr).  Ma secondo me sulla linea Gigio è il numero uno al mondo! Ha un fisico enorme e un’esplosività impressionante. Però Mike è al Milan da oltre cinque anni ormai e ha vinto pure il campionato! Anche qui parliamo di un campione e lo ha dimostrato anche con la nazionale francese, facendo molto, molto bene…”   In cuor tuo, quando Donnarumma decise di non rinnovare il contratto e di andare al PSG a parametro zero, non speravi di diventare il portiere titolare del Milan?  “Sì, certo che ci speravo, ma ero molto, molto giovane. Poi ero il terzo o quarto portiere. Se vuoi diventare il titolare del Milan devi essere un campione! Hanno preso Mike, ma io sono felice dei miei anni in rossonero. Va bene così.”   La scuola italiana dei portieri è così forte come si dice?  “L’Italia ha fatto la storia sfornando portieri di grande livello, soprattutto tecnicamente. Lì sono fissati con la tecnica… come pari la palla e come muovi i piedi. E tutte quelle cose lì… Credo che per un portiere giovane quella italiana sia una scuola fantastica."   Ma come ti ha scoperto il Milan?  "Penso tramite lo scouting… Io avevo mosso i primi passi al Vejle. Ho fatto un anno in nazionale nell’Under 17 e penso che mi abbiano scoperto lì. Ma sinceramente non sono sicurissimo. Sono stato fortunato ad andare lì e seguo tuttora il Milan. È il club per il quale tifavo da piccolo. È un grande club a livello mondiale che tifo ancora. Auguro tutto il meglio al Milan.”   Il tuo preparatore dei portieri è stato niente meno che mister Nelson Dida, vincitore di due Champions League e portiere del Grande Milan.  “Innanzitutto è una persona straordinaria! Se non sbaglio è passato dalla Primavera alla prima squadra. Anche da lui ho imparato tantissimo, così come da Gigi Ragno ed Emiliano Betti, che erano anche loro preparatori dei portieri. Però che feeling con Dida, che era una vera e propria leggenda. Sono stati bei tempi…”   Tresoldi, tra andata e ritorno fra Bruges e Westerlo nella passata stagione, ti ha segnato tre gol. Il Milan starebbe pensando di prenderlo a gennaio: farebbe bene a puntare sull’italo-tedesco? “Assolutamente sì! Lui qui in Belgio sta facendo davvero bene, è sempre tra i capocannonieri del campionato. È ancora molto giovane, ma sta facendo dei passi impressionanti. Il Milan farà bene a prenderlo.”   È quel tipo di attaccante che magari non vedi per 89 minuti e partecipa poco al gioco... e poi al 90’ la butta dentro. Sei d’accordo?  “Esatto… quando deve essere lì, è là… davanti alla porta. In fase di realizzazione è davvero forte! Il Bruges ha una squadra forte che lo manda sempre in porta! Nicolò è davvero forte!”   Quando vedi Torriani giocare in prima squadra di tanto in tanto, non provi un po’ d’invidia? Magari con un pizzico di fortuna in più avresti potuto esserci tu al suo posto…  “Sì, ma nel calcio, così come nella vita, le cose vanno come devono andare. Lui ha avuto anche adesso delle occasioni. Sono felice per lui, anche perché abbiamo fatto un po’ lo stesso percorso nel Milan. Quando lo vedo giocare sono felice per lui.”   Sogni di tornare al Milan da protagonista un giorno? Magari come un certo Dida che dopo la papera di Leeds lasciò in prestito prima di tornare e diventare il miglior portiere al mondo dal 2002 al 2005...   “Mah, certo! È un sogno e ogni giocatore sogna di giocare per una grande squadra come il Milan. È un sogno che ho da bambino… Forza Milan!”

Kieran Gibbs recalls his infamous Chelsea red card

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
