A case of mistaken identity sparked one of the most bizarre Premier League incidents ever

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Controversial refereeing decisions have been a part of football for a long as the game has been around, but one of the most bizarre incidents in recent memory occurred during Arsenal’s infamous 6-0 defeat to Chelsea in March 2014.

After spotting an Arsenal handball on the goal line, referee Andrer Marriner reached for his pocket and flashed a red card to Kieran Gibbs.

However, the issue here was that it was actually Alex Oxlade-Chamberlain who had handled the ball, and in this pre-VAR era, Gibbs was forced into an early bath, despite the protests of players from both sides.

As Gibbs looks back on the incident more than a decade on, he remembers the confusion, the frustration and the surreal nature of the incident which wouldn’t be allowed to happen these days.

“It feels like a blur now, and it did at the time too,” he reflects to FourFourTwo. “It’s heated in the moment and you’re trying to focus on the game.”

The incident came during a calamitous first half for the Gunners, who found themselves 2-0 down after just seven minutes in what was Arsene Wenger’s 1,000th game in charge of the club.

The handball incident came in the 17th minute, when Oxlade-Chamberlain threw his left hand at an Eden Hazard shot, tipping it around the post. Marriner pointed to the spot and the referee was not for turning, despite widespread protests from both sets of players.

The best features, fun and footballing quizzes, straight to your inbox every week.

The Belgian would slot home the penalty to effectively end the game as a contest, with Gibbs still finding it remarkable that such an incident would occur, given the technology now used in the game.

“It feels crazy that there was no VAR, because you don’t have those kinds of issues anymore,” he continues.

“It was a surreal and very frustrating moment that was unique in the Premier League, so it brought a lot of attention.

“Both captains and the referee were telling me I was definitely going off.

“After a few minutes of protesting, I needed to listen to them.

“It was a big error from the referee, who apologised later and the red card was rescinded.”

For more than a decade, Joe Mewis has worked in football journalism as a reporter and editor. Mewis has had stints at Mirror Football and LeedsLive among others and worked at FourFourTwo throughout Euro 2024, reporting on the tournament. In addition to his journalist work, Mewis is also the author of four football history books that include times on Leeds United and the England national team. Now working as a digital marketing coordinator at Harrogate Town, too, Mewis counts some of his best career moments as being in the iconic Spygate press conference under Marcelo Bielsa and seeing his beloved Leeds lift the Championship trophy during lockdown.

European-Japanese BepiColombo probe arrives in orbit around Mercury today

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Latest space missions, discoveries and exploration updates.

Stargazing tips, cosmic events and expert insights on the universe

Space news, cosmic updates and expert insights straight to your inbox.

Breaking space news, the latest updates on rocket launches, skywatching events and more!

Breaking space news, the latest updates on rocket launches, skywatching events and more!

Sign up to our monthly entertainment newsletter to keep up with all our coverage of the latest sci-fi and space movies, tv shows, games and books.

Discover this week's must-see night sky events, moon phases, and stunning astrophotos. Sign up for our skywatching newsletter and explore the universe with us!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

It's a moment more than eight years in the making.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Mercury is about to receive a robotic visitor from Earth.

The BepiColombo mission — a joint effort of the European Space Agency (ESA) and the Japanese Aerospace Exploration Agency (JAXA) — will arrive in orbit around Mercury today (Nov. 21), more than eight years after leaving its home planet.

The big moment will come after an orbit-insertion burn, which is scheduled to begin today at 1:24 p.m. EST (1824 GMT) and last for 37 minutes.

BepiColombo consists of two main spacecraft: ESA's Mercury Planetary Orbiter (MPO) and JAXA's Mercury Magnetospheric Orbiter (MMO), also known as Mio.

The duo launched — along with a power and propulsion element called the Mercury Transfer Module (MTM) — aboard an Ariane 5 rocket from French Guiana in October 2018, beginning a long and looping trip through the inner solar system.

Mercury is a difficult planet to reach because it's so close to the sun. Spacecraft approaching the solar system's innermost planet are accelerated to high velocities by the immense gravitational pull of our star and must shed a great deal of kinetic energy to be captured by Mercury's much weaker tug.

So the BepiColombo team designed a trajectory that featured one flyby of Earth, two of Venus and six of Mercury over the course of about seven years. That flight time was increased by a year, however, after the mission suffered an issue with its solar panels, which reduced the power available for the MTM's electric thrusters.

Today's orbital insertion is the latest milestone in BepiColombo's Mercury arrival phase, which is expected to take about six months in total.

Arrival began on Sept. 3, when the MTM released MPO and Mio. The two orbiters remain connected today; they won't separate until Dec. 9 or thereabouts.

Shortly thereafter, Mio will settle into its final orbit, a highly elliptical path that will take it over Mercury's poles. MPO is also destined for a polar orbit, though a much less elliptical one than Mio's; the ESA probe will conduct a series of engine burns before finally getting there on March 10, 2027.

BepiColombo won't start doing science work until April 6, 2027. The two probes will gather complementary data from their two different vantage points, shedding a great deal of light on a mysterious world.

"The mission will study all aspects of Mercury, from the structure and dynamics of its magnetosphere and how it interacts with the solar wind, to its internal structure with its large iron core, and the origin of the planet’s magnetic field," ESA officials wrote in a BepiColombo mission description.

"The data will enable scientists to understand more about the origin and evolution of a planet located close to its parent star, and a better understanding of the overall evolution of our solar system," they added.

Michael Wall is the Spaceflight and Tech Editor for Space.com and joined the team in 2010. He primarily covers human and robotic spaceflight, military space, and exoplanets, but has been known to dabble in the space art beat. His book about the search for alien life, "Out There," was published on Nov. 13, 2018. Before becoming a science writer, Michael worked as a herpetologist and wildlife biologist. He has a Ph.D. in evolutionary biology from the University of Sydney, Australia, a bachelor's degree from the University of Arizona, and a graduate certificate in science writing from the University of California, Santa Cruz. To find out what his latest project is, you can follow Michael on Twitter.

Best Laptops for Travel (2026): Which to Take on the Road

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

As a laptop reviewer, I have plenty of devices to choose from when I travel for work. In some cases, my favorite overall laptop doesn’t translate into the one I want to travel with. When I’m squeezed into a plane, I’ll choose maximum battery life and portability over features like a bigger screen or more ports.

Fortunately, there are many great travel laptops, and you’ll still appreciate them when you aren’t on the road. Of the hundreds of laptops I’ve tested in my life, here are the ones I always choose to travel with.

If there’s an obvious pick for the best travel laptop, it’s the Dell XPS 13. It’s been the best compact laptop you can buy for years, and that’s even more true in its latest incarnation. The key dimension to pay attention to on the XPS 13 is the depth, which is crucial for air travel. At 7.9 inches, it’s among the smallest laptops you can buy. Those economy-class seat-back tray tables on airplanes are only around 10 inches deep, and while that’s technically enough to accommodate even a 16-inch MacBook Pro, whether you can actually open the lid and use the laptop is another question. The XPS 13 is even smaller than the MacBook Neo, despite having a larger screen.

The XPS 13 achieves this by using a 13.4-inch display (measured diagonally), compared to many of the 14-inch laptops on this list. Second, it has very narrow bezels around that screen, minimizing the device’s overall footprint. While 14-inch laptops have become more popular in recent years, that bigger display size also increases the overall footprint—and every millimeter counts when working on a plane. The XPS 13 still has enough room for your palms when typing (unless you have huge hands), and the keyboard is among my favorites to type on.

The other two major advantages for travel are weight and battery life. This little laptop only weighs 2.2 pounds, a full half-pound lighter than the 13-inch MacBook Air. You might forget you have it in your backpack while traveling.

Now, the XPS 13 isn't the most powerful laptop in the world—at least not the cheaper models. The standard version comes with only 8 GB of RAM and an Intel chip with only two Performance cores. It’s good enough for basic web tasks, but for real work, you might run into limitations. You can address this by opting for the 16 GB model (which keeps the price under $1,000) or upgrading to the much more powerful Core Ultra 7 355 processor. The latter is much more expensive and comes with twice the storage, but it doesn’t add extra weight or size. Regardless of the chip you choose, the XPS 13 gets absolutely fantastic battery life. It depends on what you're doing with it, but you could easily work through a long international flight without worrying about plugging in.

If weight is your primary concern, the Asus Zenbook A14 is another contender. Despite being larger overall than the XPS 13 (with a bigger screen), the Zenbook A14 weighs 2.16 pounds—a hair lighter than the Dell. That’s seriously impressive, and I still remember how astounded I was when I first picked it up. Asus debuts its “ceraluminum” material in the chassis, which gives you the sturdiness of aluminum with the lightness of plastic. It’s not quite as rigid as metal, but it’s not as flimsy as ultra-light laptops in the past.

If you’re less worried about working on the plane and more about lugging a laptop around, the Zenbook A14 is a good choice. The Zenbook A14 has several advantages over the XPS 13 that can be helpful on the go, including built-in ports like a headphone jack, USB-A, and HDMI. If you’re working out of a remote office or presenting to a client, these can definitely come in handy. The XPS 13 only has two USB-C ports and nothing else.

There are a number of different versions of the Asus Zenbook A14 floating around out there. I’d stay away from the 8 GB model Asus sells and opt for the 16 GB model instead. There’s also a newer model that uses the Snapdragon X2 Elite chip, but I don’t think it’s worth the extra $450—especially not for a laptop focused on travel.

The 13-inch MacBook Air also makes a great travel laptop. It’s relatively small, thanks to its 13.6-inch screen.

In terms of portability, its main advantage over the XPS 13 and Zenbook A14 is its thinness. At 0.42 inches, it’s the thinnest laptop out there—and it doesn’t stick huge rubber feet on the bottom to compensate like other laptops do, such as the Razer Blade 14. You might not think thickness matters much in a travel laptop, but if you’re squeezing stuff into a backpack, every extra millimeter matters.

The MacBook Neo is also worth mentioning here. It’s not quite as thin as the MacBook Air, but because its screen is 0.4 inches smaller, it has a more compact overall footprint. Battery life is the bigger issue when taking the Neo on a trip, as it doesn’t last as long as the MacBook Air or Dell XPS 13. The 8 GB of RAM limitation doesn’t affect its use as a travel laptop much, but it's something to consider for a work laptop in general. That said, it has a very pretty screen for a laptop at this price.

Budget laptops aren’t typically known for being particularly lightweight and travel-worthy. Most of them struggle, especially with battery life. The HP OmniBook 5 might be an exception. You can usually find it on sale for under $700, even with 16 GB of RAM and an OLED display.

The OmniBook 5 isn’t quite as compact as the MacBook Neo because of its larger screen, but it is also half an inch thick and weighs only 2.8 pounds. The Snapdragon X Plus also delivers solid battery life, plenty to feel comfortable traveling with. It’s not a laptop without compromises (like the poor touchpad), but it comes with 16 GB of RAM, unlike some other laptops at this price. That’s a solid deal if you need something to travel with on a budget.

Good question. Tablets can make for great travel devices because of their size and ease of use. If you need a proper keyboard and touchpad—and the app support to handle a typical workday—you’re probably better off getting a laptop.

The Surface Pro 12 is the two-in-one laptop I’d recommend first. It’s absolutely tiny for a computer, even though it runs the full version of Windows 11. Even with the detachable keyboard connected, it weighs only 1.8 pounds. The only real problem with bringing this on a plane is the kickstand, which adds some extra depth. It’s just small enough that it should fit while open on an airplane tray table, unlike the 13.8-inch Surface Pro model.

Of course, that kickstand is also a huge advantage for the Surface Pro 12 if you’re using it without the keyboard. While that would make work difficult, it’s ideal for watching movies. Set this up on your tray table with some movies downloaded, and you’ll be the envy of your aisle. And when you need to get some work done in a coffee shop or at a smaller table, you’ll be happy to have access to the keyboard and touchpad—both of which are very comfortable to use. Just know that it's sold separately.

The iPad Air, paired with the Magic Keyboard, avoids the Surface Pro 12’s kickstand issue. And with only an 11-inch screen, it’s perfect for use in small spaces—with or without the keyboard (note that there is also a 13-inch version). If you can accomplish all your work on an iPad, more power to ya! The iPad Air should give you everything you need, so long as you get the Magic Keyboard. If your travels involve more leisurely activities, the iPad Air is a great device for reading by the pool or for playing games.

One benefit of this latest version of the iPad Air is not only its performance (thanks to the M4 chip inside) but also the front-facing camera, which has been moved to landscape for more natural-feeling video calls. Of course, you could also opt for the 11-inch iPad Pro for extra performance (and the incredible tandem OLED screen). It’s also slightly thinner and lighter, though I don't think it's worth the significant price difference.

Lastly, there’s definitely room here for laptops with 360-degree hinges—a different class of two-in-one laptops. I haven’t tested a recent Windows device with this form factor that I truly loved, but there’s no question it works well on an airplane. Just fold the screen back with the keyboard face down, and you have the perfect screen for watching movies. The Lenovo Yoga 9i 14 is a gorgeous option, though it’s incredibly expensive right now. But if you can afford it, you’ll love the 2-in-1 action combined with one of the slickest designs imaginable. The Framework Laptop 12 is also worth considering. While it’s a bit thick and heavy for a travel laptop, its smaller screen, touchscreen, and 360-degree hinge make it a good option. It’s also one of the cheaper options available, and it’s user-upgradeable.

Whether you’re packing for a trip across the world or across the state, a proper travel laptop shouldn’t be heavy or overly large. That rules out 15-inch and 16-inch laptops, which are so big you can’t use them on airplane tray tables or at coffee shops. But even when you narrow it down to 14-inch laptops or smaller, there are three portability factors to consider.

When you’re on the go, you want a laptop that doesn’t constantly make you worry about finding an outlet. Bringing a laptop power bank can help, but the biggest factor in staying sane while traveling is a device that can stay on. Many factors affect how long a laptop can last, including CPU efficiency, battery wattage, screen brightness and resolution, and more.

Long-lasting laptops, such as the Dell XPS 13, will get you through a long flight. The processor is the main thing to look out for. Qualcomm’s Snapdragon X series of chips are battery-efficient, as are Apple’s M-series chips. Intel also has some highly efficient CPUs in its most recent lineup, though you’ll need to pay close attention to the model number, as it can vary wildly depending on the specific CPU you’re using. To dig deeper, read my extensive guide on How to Buy the Right Laptop, which breaks down different CPU options.

The size of seat-back tray tables varies. There’s no exact size because of various factors, such as the airline, aircraft, cabin, and seat. Remember: Just because a laptop can physically fit on a tray table doesn’t mean you’ll have space to open and use it. With that in mind, here are the general averages to keep in mind. Airlines do not list tray table sizes, but you can expect one in economy to measure around 16 x 9 inches, while premium cabins will have larger tables depending on the seat.

I’ve included both tablets and laptops in my recommendations, but your choice will depend on why you’re traveling with a device. If it’s for a vacation and you really only need a device to read, watch, and play on (perhaps with the occasional email check), a tablet works perfectly. Lounging with a tablet, whether on a plane or by the pool, feels much more leisurely than using a laptop. More importantly, it'll help you avoid dipping back into work mode. Whether an Android tablet, iPad, or Windows tablet, just pair it with a compatible keyboard case—and you’re good to go.

However, if you’re traveling for work (or need to work while traveling), a proper laptop will be better. The one you already have may work just fine, but frequent fliers know they prefer a more compact device. And more specifically, a small laptop with a keyboard and trackpad will work better on your lap or on small desks than a tablet.

Italy U21 coach discusses Cisse’s rise, next steps for Camarda and why Bartesaghi is ‘a luxury’

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Italy’s U21 head coach, Silvio Baldini, has shared his thoughts on the Milan trio Alphadjo Cisse, Francesco Camarda and Davide Bartesaghi.

In recent years, Milan have shifted more towards giving some of their academy graduates more playing time with the first team. Bartesaghi was the first to properly get in, cementing his spot in the starting eleven last season, while Cisse has been the big breakthrough this season.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

Camarda has had a tougher time after a loan spell at Lecce, but Milan have made it very clear through renewals and words that they will focus more on the youngsters. It’s exciting times as well, given how they have all performed with the U21 national team too.

Speaking to Gazzetta dello Sport this morning, Italy’s U21 coach Silvio Baldini was asked about the breakthrough of Cisse, Camarda’s current state of mind and why Bartesaghi is a ‘luxury’ for his team. Safe to say that he likes the trio a lot.

Cisse has had a proper breakthrough. Did you see that coming?

“Compared to a year ago, I can see a different guy, who’s more mature and prepared, with higher self-esteem, psychologically changed, simply stronger.

“He has Milan to thank for signing him from Catanzaro in Serie B, believing in him and making room for him, then giving him consistent playing time. If he then makes the national team and does well, so much the better.”

Milan deserve credit, and you also deserve credit for discovering him as a centre-forward…

“Analysing training sessions, a series of situations emerged where he managed to score by attacking space and depth: if you can do it on the right and left, it means you can also do it in the centre. But even there, the credit goes entirely to the staff.

“They had the intuition, realising that Cisse could be useful to our game system in that way and at the same time, allowing Alpha to discover a new role successfully. I’m simply honest and meritocratic in my choices.

“Even though the goal against Armenia actually came from him as a second striker, at the end of that match we had two centre-forwards, him and Camarda.”

Camarda, by the way, has struggled at Milan but scored for the U21s. How is that possible?

“I can only speak about the work we do together. I spoke to him at length, I dedicated time to him, I asked him to explain his concerns, his fear of not being able to express himself as he would like. I told him stories of my own life, of how I came out of it.

“A dialogue not as a coach-player but as a son, or rather a grandson, as he could be, and a grandfather: from the heart. I’m not interested in performances as an end in themselves; they will come if he feels in the right place.

“I try to make him understand that I see him, I follow him in many small ways: he’s a conscientious boy who shouldn’t become obsessed with the goal, but rather experience football for what it is: fun.

“If, instead of fun, it becomes a test, then it worries you, and if you worry, you no longer enjoy it and it doesn’t help you perform at your best.”

To have more fun, do you recommend that he should leave Milan for another team?

“I’m not going to get involved in this. There are clubs, agents, and a family: they know what the best path will be for him. Let’s remember that he’s 18 years old, he plays for AC Milan and in the Under-21s.

“It’s clear that the more he plays, the better it will be for AC Milan and for everyone. We’re talking about a great player, a champion in my opinion, who needs to finish his development: he’s still growing, he already has a great eye for goal, and he’s sacrificing himself for the team.”

Camarda-Cisse: Can Milan provide the national team with one of the forward duos of the future?

“For me, yes. I see them as a permanent fixture in the national team, and of course they need to prove it and earn it on the pitch. But they have all the characteristics to do it. I found Alpha to be better prepared, even in his game decisions. Amorim did well to develop him.”

The third Rossoneri player is Bartesaghi: more of a full-back like with you or a winger like at Milan?

“Davide is one of the best players in his position I’ve coached in many years. He’s talented, very professional, mature, introverted yet open, and that’s not a contradiction: he has enormous potential, and he shows it every time he comes to us.

“If I were any Serie A coach, I’d take him with me. For the Under-21s, Bartesaghi is a luxury. He plays with heart, passion, and intelligence, as well as possessing high-level technical, physical, and running skills.

“He can play in any position on the flank. In my opinion, if he starts 50 meters from the goal, he can perform at his best.”

T-Mobile is giving away the iPhone 18 Pro for free this weekend — how to qualify

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

TL;DR: This Black Friday, the Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free when you switch to T-Mobile and bring your number on a qualifying T-Mix plan.

Black Friday deals don't actually wait for Black Friday. Instead, major retailers and mobile carriers drop heaps of early discounts throughout November — which is great news if you desperately need a tech upgrade without the wait.

Why wait until Nov. 27 when T-Mobile is already offering one of the strongest mobile promos of the season?

We’ve rounded up all the best Black Friday deals from T-Mobile, but the clear standout is a limited-time offer that gets you the Apple iPhone 18 Pro for free (100% off its $1,199 list price). Apple rarely offers direct cash discounts on current-generation Pro models during Cyber Week, and certainly never takes $1,199 off an unlocked handset. That makes this early offer is best way to upgrade right now.

T-Mobile keeps things surprisingly flexible when it comes to qualifying for this deal:

Switch without a trade-in: Port your existing phone number from an eligible rival carrier to a qualifying T-Mix plan, and T-Mobile covers the full cost of the phone.

Trade in a phone in any condition: Already a T-Mobile customer or adding a new line without porting a number? You can still get the iPhone 18 Pro for free by trading in an eligible phone in any condition on a qualifying T-Mix plan.

T-Mobile distributes the full discount as 36 monthly bill credits. If you leave T-Mobile or cancel your line before that three-year payment plan wraps up, the credits stop immediately, and you will owe the remaining balance on the device.

You also won’t walk out of the checkout process without paying anything. You are still required to pay local sales tax on the phone's full $1,199.00 retail value upfront, plus T-Mobile's standard $35 device connection fee per line at checkout. You can't escape those upfront charges, even on "free" Black Friday phone promos.

Score the Apple iPhone 18 Pro for free this weekend with T-Mobile.

Apple iPads are better than ever. We know because we've tested them all, from the older base model iPad to the brand-new iPad Mini. It has crisp displays for brilliant graphics, and one of its most surprising upgrades in the 2026 model is more memory. Now with 12GB of RAM, it's better equipped as a gaming device, too. Essentially, it gives you everything you'd want in a starter MacBook, but it costs you just $749 and has a touchscreen.

Budget shoppers: If you prefer saving money over splurging on the latest tech, the Apple iPad (11th generation) gets the job done. Best for entertainment: If you're not interested in working on your iPad, then the iPad Mini is our top recommendation for you. The best Kindles of 2026: We tested Amazon’s new e-readers to find the winners

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In October, Amazon launched a brand-new round of Kindle e-readers, making it even harder to pick one. It doesn't help that the new 2026 Kindles are more expensive, too. I've spent years testing Kindles for Mashable, and I can tell you that the latest batch is impressive, and it shows exactly why Kindles are still the most popular e-readers.

After testing the newest Kindles, we have a new favorite. The Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition has edged out the standard Paperwhite as the best Kindle to buy in 2026. However, that doesn't mean it's the best Kindle for everyone, and so we've tested the whole Kindle lineup to make expert recommendations for every type of reader.

These are the tech, tools, and products — from laptops to e-readers, from earbuds to robovacs, and more — that Mashable ranks best in class.

Once you have a Kindle, there's still so much you can do to make the most of it. For avid readers, Stuff Your Kindle Day and Libby let you cross every last book off your annual reading list. You can even buy Kindle accessories like remote page-turners and gooseneck stands so you don't have to hold your Kindle while reading. (By the way, we love Amazon's new Kindle Click and Page Turn Cover, and the latter even reminds us of the cult-favorite Kindle Oasis, RIP.)

So, after extensive testing, reading so many books, checking in with BookTok authors, and attending BookCon, I created this guide to the best Kindles of 2026. Plus, with Black Friday coming up, I've found the best deals on Kindles.

Yes, Kindles are on sale for the Amazon Black Friday sale, so if you're buying an e-reader as a Christmas gift, this is your chance to save. Amazon kicked off its Black Friday sale on Nov. 19, and with it, all of the 2024 Kindle models dropped in price. The best deal is on the 2024 Kindle Paperwhite, one of our favorites. Fortunately, we still love the 2024 Kindles, and they even work with the new Kindle Click.

On Oct. 1, Amazon announced a redesign of the entire Kindle collection. The new Kindles have vivid new colors like ube, and for the first time, you can buy these e-readers with aluminum casings. The new Kindles are also thinner and lighter than before. They also have a removable back panel so you can eventually replace the battery, though we have had some problems with this feature.

Most readers: Amazon's latest release, the Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition, is the best Kindle. It turns pages without stuttering, never slowing down when you’re in the thick of the action.

People who read on the go: The 7-inch screen has adjustable brightness and warmth but it also auto-adjusts. That means if you're commuting or the sun changes overhead, the Paperwhite Signature Edition will adjust the screen based on your environment. The 2026 model is lighter and thinner than ever and fits comfortably in small crossbody bags. Plus, it’s waterproof, keeping it safe from poolside vacations and spilled water bottles in bags.

When picking the best Kindle, it always comes down to this question — Paperwhite or Paperwhite Signature Edition? Historically, I've preferred the Paperwhite, finding that the Signature Edition doesn't have enough features to justify its price. The 2026 Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition finally fixes that. With a new aluminum case, the Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition feels like a high-end piece of tech. With a thinner body and square edges, this new Kindle feels more like a smartphone or tablet than it does an e-reader.

But inside the more advanced chassis is still the same e-reader you love. It's fast with a high contrast 300ppi resolution. The matte display is glare-free and easy on the eyes. With adjustable warmth and brightness you can read with it in bright summer sun at the beach and then turn it down and warm it up when you're in a dim bedroom. And speaking of the beach, its IPX8 rating makes it safe to use in the bath or at the pool.

It is a more expensive e-reader at $249.99, especially compared to the Paperwhite's $199.99, but here's what you get for an extra $50. The upgraded aluminum case makes that $50 nearly worth it compared to the plastic exterior of the Paperwhite, but the Signature Edition also has an ad-free lockscreen, 32GB of storage compared to the Paperwhite's 16GB, wireless charging, auto-adjusting brightness, and best of all, compatibility with the new Kindle Page Turn Cover.

Plus, the Kindle Paperwhite Signature comes in more exciting color options. While the Paperwhite just comes in graphite, the Signature Edition also comes in a stunning seaglass green.

Budget shoppers: Especially in light of the Kindle price increases, if you want to read books on an e-reader but need a budget-friendly model, the regular Kindle is the best option. But with a budget device, keep in mind you have to make some sacrifices. The basic Kindle isn't waterproof nor does it have adjustable warm light.

Amazon made some updates to the 2026 Kindle that make it worthy of its higher price tag. It has a new, flush front design for a sleeker look. Plus, the design team made a change we've been dying for — they moved the power button to the side of the device. Plenty of specs have stayed the same between generations. It still has a 6-inch screen, 300 ppi resolution, and six weeks of battery life.

What truly makes it stand out is just how lightweight it is. The 2026 Kindle weighs 18 grams (.66 ounces) less than the previous generation and it's a noticeable difference. It feels like you're barely holding anything; plus, it's even better for travel as it takes up very little space and weighs just half a pound.

It's a speedy little e-reader, too. Its screen is responsive and loads books within seconds. The only thing that could make the e-reader better is if Amazon added an adjustable warm light and made it waterproof. However, according to Amazon, the lack of those features keeps it affordable.

The device is now available in its basic format in either ube or graphite. However, it's also available with an aluminum backing, making it more like a tablet. The aluminum Kindle is more expensive at $189.99, but it does come with 32GB of storage.

If you don't like the new prices for Amazon's new 2026 Kindles, then you can still buy the 2024 Kindle Paperwhite. Up until October, this was our pick as the best Kindle, and it's just as good now. Best of all, during big sales like Black Friday, you can pick up this popular e-reader for $129.99. The new model costs $199.99, which is a big ask for most readers.

The screen's front light is vibrant and has adjustable brightness, meaning it can adjust when transitioning from reading in the sun or shade. It also comes with adjustable screen warmth, perfect for evening readers who want to give their eyes a chance to relax. It’s lightweight and fits comfortably in small crossbody bags. Plus, it’s waterproof, keeping it safe against poolside vacations and spilled water bottles in bags.

I used this Kindle for two years, and it's as good as the day I took it out of the box. Plus, it doesn't suffer from the back cover design flaw readers have said they've encountered with the 2026 Kindle e-readers.

There's a recurring question we see about Kindles: What’s the difference between the Kindle Paperwhite and Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition? The Signature Edition has twice the storage of the Paperwhite (32GB), auto-adjusting screen brightness, an ad-free lockscreen, and wireless charging, and it costs $40 more. Sure, those things are helpful, but I don’t think those extra features are necessary.

Not a ton changed between the 2022 and 2024 models of the Kindle Paperwhite, but we can be certain that the 2024 Kindle Paperwhite is the sleeker, faster e-reader. The 2024 Paperwhite got a slight upgrade in screen quality compared to the 2022 Paperwhite. While the resolution is still 300 ppi, it appears to have higher contrast, straying away from the like-paper look and edging closer to the look of a tablet. Don’t worry, though — unlike tablets, the Kindle Paperwhite still has a gloriously glare-free screen. It still has an IPX68 waterproof rating, so you can get some leisurely reading in the bath.

Note: On Oct. 1, 2026, Amazon released a new version of the Kindle Paperwhite Kids. We are currently in the process of testing the new e-reader.

Young readers: If you're raising a voracious reader, the Kindle Paperwhite Kids is the best Kindle for kids. It's waterproof, which means it stands up to juice spills and dips in the pool. Plus, it comes with a Kindle case included. It's designed to be kid-proof, not just physically, but also with built-in parental controls. If you're concerned about monitoring your kid's bookshelf, this Kindle allows you to approve downloads by kids and displays age-friendly titles that kids can easily access.

More than anything else, a Kindle for kids has to be waterproof, which is why the Kindle Paperwhite Kids is the best choice. It comes with everything we like about the standard Paperwhite: a beautiful, high-resolution screen that works faster than any previous generation.

But what makes it great for kids is that it's a distraction-free device. It doesn't come with ads, notifications, or the temptation of games or video. I also like that it's a bit heavier than the standard Paperwhite, giving it a sturdier design. Plus, the built-in case adds an extra level of protection.

The parental controls are easy to use and set up, and the device also comes with a six-month free subscription to Amazon Kids+, which provides unlimited access to thousands of age-appropriate books. The library of books is easy for kids to navigate and offers classics like Lois Lowry's The Giver and modern classics like Diary of a Wimpy Kid.

What I don't love is the price, especially now that it just rose by $50. At $229.99, it's more expensive than the standard Paperwhite. If you want to go for a budget kids' pick, I'd go for a standard Kindle, which you can set up as a kids' device during setup to ensure parental controls and add an Amazon Kids+ subscription for $5.99 per month. Plus, as your kid ages, you won't have to upgrade to a more teen-friendly e-reader.

For color enthusiasts: The Kindle Colorsoft would probably be a better option for kids than for adults, serving as a bridge between colorful storybooks and more challenging chapter books. But it works for adults too, especially those who want color or to use the Page Turn Cover.

The Kindle Colorsoft makes some modest improvements over its predecessor, introducing the same design changes as the rest of Amazon's 2026 Kindle lineup. That means the new Kindle Colorsoft has a slimmer build, magnetic Pogo pins, and edge-to-edge color, so the bezels now match the device's color. Overall, it's an improvement, but it doesn't solve the problems of the original device, nor fully justify the $289.99 price (or $319.99 for the Signature Edition).

Unfortunately, E Ink technology has extremely dim and muted colors. Even if you crank the vibrancy settings all the way to the max, the colors are disappointing. When you compare the Kindle Colorsoft side-by-side with graphic novels or books with illustrations, the differences are extremely obvious. If you want to read comics or similar color artwork digitally, you're better off with a tablet.

With those major caveats, if you want a Kindle with color, this is the best option. On the plus side, we love the fig colorway (it matches the burgundy iPhone), and the color lockscreen artwork does look great.

We have yet to thoroughly vet the new Kindle Paperwhite, but before the device's release, we got a sneak peek of the new models. We were impressed by the new aluminum models of the Kindle Paperwhite. Plus, I'm pleased to see that the power button has been moved to the side of the device. Based on how much we've loved previous generations of the Kindle Paperwhite, we feel comfortable saying the new gen will be a good purchase.

Read our full review of the Kindle Scribe (3rd Generation).

Note takers and annotators: If, looking through your bookshelf, your books have dog-eared pages, highlighted lines, and scribbles in the margins, your instinct might be that an e-reader isn't for you, but I'd like to introduce you to the Kindle Scribe. It's the only Kindle with writing capabilities and comes with an included stylus pen that magnetically attaches to the side of the e-reader; it's ready for all your handwritten thoughts.

The pen moves nicely across the screen, mimicking the texture of writing on paper. Its larger screen also makes it better for writing and reading PDFs.

In addition to a library of books, the Kindle Scribe also comes with a journal section where you can take notes. The notes feature is especially great for students who want to handwrite notes, convert their handwriting into text, and manage documents through Google Drive.

The 2025 Kindle Scribe got a much-needed upgrade. All the good stuff has held over with the addition of some major improvements.

It's still big, with an 11-inch display, which does make it a little more awkward to hold while reading, but for writing or annotating, it's optimal. Unlike other tablets (I'm looking at you, iPads) that require you to buy a pen separately, the Kindle Scribe comes with a pen that conveniently attaches to the side so it doesn't get lost. However, it's not the most secure magnetic hold, so be careful not to bump the pen off accidentally.

Writing on the Kindle Scribe is a joy. Before testing this e-reader, I used my iPad and Apple Pencil to annotate readings, but I never loved it because the screen felt too slick for the Apple Pencil. The Kindle Scribe does not suffer this problem, as the matte screen lends some friction to the pen, making it feel like you're writing on paper. I enjoyed writing with the pen, and I swear my handwriting looked better on the Kindle Scribe than on my iPad.

It finally got a processing update that makes it as speedy as every other Kindle. Gone is the lag when switching between journals and books. Plus, the displays have greatly improved, too, as the 2025 Kindle Scribe has virtually no ghosting. Like the Kindle Paperwhite, the Scribe's battery life lasts 12 weeks. It also has multiple storage options, as you can choose between 32GB or 64GB.

It's not your only option if you want a Kindle that can write. The Scribe Colorsoft is also available, and while we appreciate that model, it's quite a bit more expensive. The 2025 Scribe is a better value, it just doesn't have the wow factor of color.

For years, Kindle prices have stayed stable, but in August 2026, Amazon finally increased prices. Every model except the Kindle Scribe received a price hike. We can thank RAMageddon, as an Amazon spokesperson told Mashable that the increases were necessary due to rising memory and component costs.

Our advice? If you're on a budget, the 2024 Kindles are still very good e-readers.

If you're concerned about buying the newest tech, that's not an issue since, as of October 2026, there are nine new models to shop.

Before you choose a Kindle, you need to ask one question first: Do you want it to be waterproof? If you prefer reading near a body of water — whether in the bath, by the pool, or at the beach — that immediately narrows down your options. Since the Kindle Oasis is no longer available, your waterproof options are the Kindle Paperwhite, Paperwhite Signature Edition, Colorsoft Signature Edition, and Paperwhite Kids.

Beyond water resistance, here are a few other features to consider:

Display size: The basic Kindle has a 6-inch display, while the largest has a 10.2-inch display.

Storage capacity: Kindles range from 16GB to 32GB of storage capacity.

Battery life: The new Kindles generally have outstanding batteries. On the low end, they last up to six weeks per charge, while others last up to 12 weeks.

Writing capability: The Kindle Scribe is the only model that allows you to write with the included stylus.

The latest Kindles all boast a maximum brightness of 94 nits and a 300 ppi, high-resolution display with zero glare, so you can enjoy sharp text and images at any viewing angle. Kindles are also equipped with WiFi connectivity, allowing you to download all the reading material you could ever want. Plus, you can listen to audiobooks on Audible via the built-in Bluetooth connection.

And if you were a die-hard Kindle Oasis fan and are attached to page-turning buttons, your best option is to get the Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition with the Page Turn Cover.

Out of all the e-readers I've tested (and I've tested a lot), Kindles have the best compatibility with Libby. I love the digital platform because I can read for free and support my local library. Some other e-readers require you to download your library e-book to a computer and then upload it to an e-reader, but with Kindles, your e-books are sent straight to your device with a click.

Libby allows you to read free e-books on a Kindle. Through Libby, you can borrow e-books through libraries, and all you need is a library card. Inside the Libby app, select the "Read with Kindle" option when you borrow a book, which allows you to send these e-books to your Kindle library.

Another option for finding free e-books is through Stuff Your Kindle events. These are author-driven events that offer limited-time deals on e-books, often making them heavily discounted or totally free.

I've read hundreds of free Kindle books with Libby. Here's how.

What is Kindle Rewards? Earn free credits when shopping the Kindle store

What is Kindle+? A library of books at your fingertips for just $12 a month.

The Mashable team is split on this question. Our resident Kindle expert Samantha Mangino (yours truly) recommends most people opt for the ad-supported version. However, Mashable Tech Editor Timothy Beck Werth recommends paying a little more to get a Kindle without lockscreen ads. Though this increases the price by $20 to $25, you won't be subjected to annoying ads for books every time you lock your e-reader. Instead, you'll see black-and-white photographs of writing implements and other book motifs.

When you're buying a Kindle, you'll notice that Amazon selects the "Lockscreen Ad Supported" version by default. Shoppers must manually select "Without Lockscreen Ads" if they want to remove advertisements from the lock screen. This increases the cost of the base Kindle from $149.99 to $169.99; the Kindle Paperwhite increases from $199.99 to $219.99. For serious readers who hate ads, we think it's worth the added cost.

In the past, the Paperwhite has been Amazon's flagship e-reader, with the Paperwhite Signature Edition being the more luxe option. However, with the 2026 redesign, it seems like the script has been flipped, with Amazon pushing the Signature Edition while the standard Paperwhite takes a back seat.

Plenty remains the same between the devices. They have the same 7-inch screen with 300 ppi resolution and adjustable warmth and brightness. Both are waterproof with IPX8 ratings and 12-week battery life. The new devices also have easy-to-open back panels to make replacing the battery that much easier.

The Paperwhite is the cheaper option at $199.99, but it comes with only 16GB of storage (which, to be fair, is still plenty) and is available in graphite with a plastic casing. The Signature Edition is more expensive at $249.99, but comes with 32GB of storage and is available in the aluminum finish in seaglass green and graphite. Beyond its nicer finish, it also has an ad-free lockscreen, automatic front-light adjustment, a rotating screen, and wireless charging. The real perk of the Paperwhite Signature Edition is its compatibility with the Page Turn Cover, which adds page-turning buttons to your Kindle.

As of May 20, 2026, Amazon no longer provides software updates or web services to devices released in 2012 or earlier, essentially bricking them. Users will still be able to read currently downloaded books on the device, but to add new books, they will need to connect the device to a computer. This is a huge hit, especially to Libby users who need web service access to read borrowed books.

Owners of these Kindle models are obviously upset, as it means they'll need to purchase a new device to maintain access to all features. While some are considering switching to a different e-reader, like a Kobo, Amazon still makes great e-readers, and it's a testament to the brand that devices that are at least 14 years old are still going strong.

Unfortunately, while a lot of Amazon devices get regular discounts throughout the year, we only see Kindle discounts during big shopping events like Prime Day and Black Friday. However, even at full price, these devices earn a strong recommendation from Mashable.

Kindle Kids editions come with built-in parental controls. It displays a library geared toward kid-friendly and age-appropriate content. Plus, it allows your kid to request books to read that you have to approve in the Kindle app — that way, you know exactly what they're reading.

Unlike other Kindle models, the Kindle Kids includes a built-in cover and a free one-year Amazon Kids+ subscription that provides access to thousands of books for kids ages 6 to 12. Plus, it comes with a worry-free guarantee: If the Kindle Kids gets damaged within two years of purchase, they'll replace it totally free.

However, any Kindle can technically be a suitable device for kids. When setting up a Kindle, you'll receive a prompt asking if the device is for children, which enables parental controls on it.

Yes, you can buy new Kindles at Best Buy. However, if you're searching for the best price and a convenient shopping experience, we recommend sticking with Amazon. Since the Kindle store is also hosted at Amazon, you'll need an account there anyway, and it's hard to beat Amazon's prices on its own products.

To select the best Kindles of 2026, I assessed the current lineup of Kindle devices. I tested each of these e-readers to get a comprehensive view of each device. I also compared them to the other e-readers I've tested.

I made sure to pinpoint which models are waterproof for a worry-free outdoor reading experience as well as those better suited for children.

Kindle models vary wildly in price; I made sure to include an affordable Kindle as well as splurge-worthy, treat-yourself picks.

This includes auto-adjusting displays for indoor/outdoor reading, warm light settings to block blue light, and page turn buttons for one-handed reading.

Andoni Iraola should snub Liverpool duo and start secret weapon vs Man United, the data is telling

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Correspondent AUTHORITY NCTJ-qualified football journalist with experience at VAVEL, Hayters TV, SPORTbible, and Centredevils. FOCUS Premier League analysis, tactical deep-dives, and original opinion content across Arsenal, Chelsea, Tottenham, Manchester City, and Celtic. THE INSIGHT Evan utilises a network of club and industry contacts to deliver verified, high-speed reporting. He provides specialist tactical and analytical content to ensure fans get beyond the headlines and understand the full picture.

Cody Gakpo is Andoni Iraola's secret weapon ahead of Liverpool's clash against Manchester United on Sunday.

The 27-year-old has a superb record against the Red Devils, and will hope to add to it at Anfield.

But it is no guarantee that the Netherlands international is selected, with the competition on both wings intense this season.

Iraola will almost certainly deploy Bradley Barcola on the left of the attack, but it remains to be seen if Gakpo will be his pick for the right with strong competition from Rio Ngumoha and Victor Munoz.

Sunday's game will be significant for both bragging rights and for Liverpool to kick off their winter fixture list with victory, but for another reason, too.

Mauricio Pochettino will be taking charge of Man United for the first time at Anfield, and the Reds will hope to treat him to a baptism of fire.

The first time Gakpo ever came across Man United was during the 2022-23 season, just two months after he had arrived in the Premier League.

The winger opened the scoring that day, and would add the third goal in what turned into a 7-0 romping of Erik ten Hag's team at Anfield.

He has netted three goals since against the Red Devils, with all of them coming in the last three fixtures between the pair.

Surprisingly, Liverpool have not won any of those games despite the clear gap between the sides in the Premier League table, and they will hope to put that right on Sunday.

But it remains to be seen if Gakpo plays at all, with his position under threat from two of the next generation.

Both Munoz and Ngumoha have enjoyed strong seasons, and bring a youthful energy to the right side of the pitch.

Yet Gakpo's record and experience make him an equally strong option, and ultimately the way to go for Iraola.

It has been an interesting few months for Gakpo, who went close to joining Manchester City in the final days of the transfer window.

He ultimately stayed and started the season excellently, before tailing off in recent weeks following an injury.

Gakpo has retained Iraola's support, and Sunday's game could well be one to jumpstart him back into form.

"I would probably say Cody Gakpo," Iraola said last month when asked about Liverpool's most underrated player.

"He’s a player who has been putting up numbers, playing and performing for many years, and sometimes he isn’t valued because he’s just there and you take him for granted. His consistency, his personality. I would probably go with Cody."

When will Cristiano Ronaldo score his 1000th career goal? Exact date and game predicted

📅 Nov 21, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Cristiano Ronaldo is now just 14 goals away from reaching 1,000 goals, the landmark he has openly targeted as the defining achievement of the last years of his career. It now becomes his final challenge following his announcement that he’ll retire from the Portugal national team.

At 41, the question is no longer whether Ronaldo can reach the four-figure mark. It is how long it will take him to get there.

The Portuguese superstar remains prolific for Al-Nassr, having taken his tally to 985 with a hat-trick in a 5-3 victory over Al Kholood on October 29th.

He recently confirmed that his 23-year international career is over, announcing his decision in an emotional farewell message to Portugal’s supporters.

“The time has come to hang up our national team’s number 7 shirt,” Ronaldo wrote, admitting that it had not been an easy decision.

That leaves Al-Nassr as his only remaining route to the 1,000-goal milestone, barring any change of heart over his international retirement or a farewell appearance for Portugal.

We’ve crunched the numbers and mapped out the various scenarios.

“I want to reach 1,000 goals,” Ronaldo said, unveiling his last major career ambition on Rio Ferdinand’s YouTube channel just over two years ago.

“If I don’t have any injuries, this for me is the most important [thing], I want that. For me, the best mark that I can have in football is to reach, first, 900 goals. After, my challenge is to be at 1,000 goals.

“All the goals I have scored, they have video. They all have video. Listen, I respect all of them [Pele and Di Stefano]. And if you want more goals, I can bring them from training, too. And I will prove to the people after. They prefer this player, or this is the best one. I don’t care about that.”

Ronaldo has maintained the importance of that ambition, previously identifying it alongside winning another Nations League title as a major motivation. The latter is now off the table, but his pursuit of four figures remains very much alive.

“One thousand goals, yes, and the national team. Winning the Nations League, the competition starts tomorrow [Thursday], it’s a new age.”

With all that in mind, here’s our calculations for when Ronaldo could potentially reach the milestone of 1000 goals in his professional career.

– Barring any major career-ending injuries, Ronaldo still looks likely to reach the milestone of a thousand goals at some point in 2027. Possibly as early as January or February, particularly if he maintains his recent scoring form.

– It’s possible that if Ronaldo enjoys a resurgence and goes on a hot streak akin to his most prolific peak at Real Madrid, he could do it before the end of the year. His goal against Al Ahli took him to 986, leaving him with 14 to find, although his missed penalty in the same game was a reminder that even the most reliable goalscorers have off days. He would still need 14 goals in little over a month, though.

– If you take his recent rate of scoring (27 goals in 42 appearances during the calendar year of 2026) as what he’ll likely maintain, another 22 appearances would be required to score the remaining 14 goals. That would take us through to around February or March 2027. Crucially, that calculation previously allowed for further Portugal appearances, which can no longer be taken for granted.

– There is an argument that Ronaldo could be set to improve upon that scoring rate. He’s been written off on the international stage after poor showings in the last two major summer tournaments, but he scored eight goals in nine games in the 2024-25 Nations League and a further five goals in qualifying for the 2026 World Cup. Although his international career is now over, rediscover that form in the Saudi Pro League, up his rate to around 1.00 goals per game and fill his boots for Al-Nassr, and Ronaldo could conceivably reach four figures early in 2027. At that rate, he’d need just 14 more appearances.

– There is a counter-argument that Ronaldo will instead slow down in these final furlongs of his career. His latest outing against Al Ahli offered evidence of both sides of that argument: he scored, but also missed a penalty in a 3-1 defeat. His 27 goals in 42 appearances in 2026 work out at 0.64 goals per game. Maintain that rate, and he’ll need another 22 games to score the 14 goals required to reach number one thousand. That should be comfortably achievable before the end of Al-Nassr’s 2026-27 campaign, which is due to end in late May 2027.

– The biggest unknown is now how prolific Ronaldo can remain at club level. His international retirement means he’ll no longer have the opportunity to add goals in Portugal colours, although it should also give him more time to concentrate on Al-Nassr and potentially manage his workload. Whether that makes him even more prolific in Saudi Arabia is another matter.

– What kind of form he’ll show to get there is ultimately anyone’s guess. His goal against Al Ahli was his second in consecutive outings after the hat-trick against Al Kholood, but the missed penalty and defeat offered a less convincing reminder of his current form. The end of his international career removes one variable from our projections, while leaving the exact date far from certain.

– We’ll hedge our bets and estimate that Ronaldo’s form over the coming months will be somewhere between his spectacular peak and a severe decline. He is playing in the Saudi Pro League, after all, and his team is set up to feed him chances. With no more international fixtures to factor into the equation, we’re backing him to keep ticking along for Al-Nassr. With just 14 goals left to score, and having found the net again despite a frustrating evening against Al Ahli, we’re sticking with February 2027 as our tentative prediction for Ronaldo’s 1,000th career goal, although the loss of potential Portugal appearances could yet push that date back.

S&P 500 correction: Five forces behind the market sell-off

📅 2026-11-20  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Esteemed Wharton professor Jeremy Siegel was more animated than usual on Friday afternoon... with good reason.

The normally staid author of "Stocks for the Long Run" spoke to CNBC on Friday as the market careened toward some ugly milestones. The S&P 500 and Dow Jones Industrial Average closed in correction territory, off more than 10% from their records. The Nasdaq Composite lost its gain for the year and the more concentrated Nasdaq-100 entered bear market territory on an intraday basis.

"These things are like straws that break the camel's back. You know what I mean? Everyone [is] all, 'I don't care, I don't care cause everything else is good' and when those straws keep on going... Bang," said Siegel.

Just a month-and-a-half ago, the S&P 500 and the Nasdaq Composite were at record highs. But the straws were already adding up by then and a market in denial is just now recognizing them.

Here are the reasons behind the sell-off:

The breaking point came this week in the form of the initial public offering of artificial intelligence leader Anthropic and a pick-up in the selling intensity for one-time AI chip leader Nvidia.

Anthropic upped its offering amount a day before going public despite the already dimming climate around AI-related stocks. The move ensured it would be the biggest IPO ever, but played into the fears that this debut — following SpaceX's monster offering over the summer — was a collective cash-out moment for the AI complex that will leave the investing masses holding the bag.

In other words, it could be the ultimate "sell the news" moment for the AI trade.

On Thursday, Anthropic traded from its $83 IPO price to a high above $115 before instantly rolling over and taking the AI trade with it. Anthropic's sell-off intensified Friday, and when the dust settled Anthropic closed the week at $88.91.

The Nasdaq posted its worst week since the Covid plunge of March 2020, down 10%.

Nvidia fell 14% on Friday with the selling intensifying after a Reuters headline hit the tape that the chipmaker's accounting is the focus of a Securities and Exchange Commission probe.

Nvidia is now down nearly 40% in November alone, a month that saw the AI bull market's leading salesman, Jensen Huang, resign as its CEO because of an affair with an alleged Chinese spy that raised speculation about whether the company's chips were ending up in Beijing's hands despite government restrictions.

To start the week, the Commerce Department tightened restrictions on U.S.-made chips to stop China from accessing them remotely from other countries.

A solid earnings report on Tuesday fell flat as Nvidia's board definitively ruled out any chance Huang would return, disappointing some of the stock's fanatics still holding out hope for his far-fetched return. Nvidia ended the week with a 23% loss.

The S&P 500 traded below its 200-day moving average — what's considered a measure of a security's long-term trend — on Tuesday and made a convincing break of it on Thursday. This turned many on Wall Street— like commodity trading advisors — into automatic sellers on the market. So-called CTAs are individuals or organizations that advise others on trading futures and swaps across a range of assets.

"We know there's a lot of trend riders on these markets," said Siegel. "They have their trend lines. They've ridden it up and as soon as it goes down, they're selling and they're selling and they're selling and they just perpetuate" the decline, he said.

The Iran conflict has caused the price oil to surge and while it's off its highs it still remains above $83 a barrel, while the fees for transiting the Strait of Hormuz and the rest of the region keep persistent pressure on global prices.

A series of numbers earlier in the month showed inflation is still far from the Federal Reserve's 2% target, sparking a run of central bank officials to warn they may have to get more aggressive with interest rates.

After increasing rates in September by a quarter point, the Fed skipped a hike in late October and sparked concern among bond investors it may be falling behind the inflation fight. The 10-year Treasury yield subsequently jumped above 5% and stayed there until a flight of safe haven fixed income buying brought it back below that level on Friday.

Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller said this week that the Fed may need to do a half percentage point hike next month to catch up.

"I'm very, very concerned about inflation. It's kind of headed in the wrong direction, and it's spreading out," Waller told CNBC.

"Don't fight the Fed" has long been a maxim on Wall Street and right now expectations are that the central bank will need to raise rates in December and again in January.

And late-breaking on Friday was a report from the Financial Times that the Trump Administration is considering restarting strikes on Iran.

Before the grim market milestones were met on Friday, it had long lost its mojo on fears that the pace of growth driven by AI companies was slowing.

As Anthropic prepared to go public, its third-quarter figures released earlier this month showed revenue growth last quarter was 47%, down from 144% in the second quarter.

AI momentum trades, like the VanEck Semiconductor ETF and the iShares MSCI USA Momentum Factor ETF, all topped out during the summer.

The fears of slowing growth picked up this month after the Democrats won control of Congress and key governorships, fueling speculation that states — or even the whole country — could implement stricter rules on data center construction.

CoreWeave, which builds data centers using high amounts of debt, is down 71% from its high. Caterpillar, which has reinvented itself as an AI play because of data center construction and generators, is down more than 50% from its high.

The view of Siegel and many market observers is that the economy remains in good enough shape that this will just be a run-of-the-mill correction that resets a bull market that had raised people's expectations too high because of its early ferocity.

The S&P 500 returned more than 20% in 2023 and 2024 and then gained 16% last year.

"The average over long periods for the stock market is 8%, 9% a year up," said Siegel. "People began to think that 15 to 20 [percent] was going to be the norm with AI and we have to bring that down to reality."

The only problem with that theory is that the stock market and its wealth effect was one of the major factors underpinning the economy. Stocks are a greater source of household wealth — especially for baby boomers — than they ever have been before.

With the S&P 500 clinging to a 1% gain for the year, this stock market rout could end up tipping off a virtuous cycle that slows the economy, and in turn, leads to bigger equity losses.

Harry Kane's Bayern blow: England captain ruled out for MONTHS after tearing ankle ligaments - but Ballon d'Or winner sends upbeat message from hospital

📅 2026-11-20  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Published: 17:58 EST, 20 November 2026 | Updated: 06:31 EST, 21 November 2026

Bayern Munich fear Harry Kane will be ruled out for months after suffering an ankle injury in their Bundesliga draw against Cologne - with the striker revealing he will only return for 'the business end of the season'.

Kane was forced off just 20 minutes into the match at the Allianz Arena following a challenge from Joel Schmied.

The defender had won the ball but caught Kane with his follow through on his ankle.

Kane had initially tried to play on after treatment, before eventually needing to be replaced.

The Ballon d'Or winner was upbeat on Saturday morning however, sharing a message with his fans that stressed his readiness to come back better than ever.

'The highs and lows of life!' Kane captioned a picture of himself wearing a protective boot. 'Obviously very disappointed to get this injury but as always it's an opportunity to show resilience and improvement going in 2027.

Harry Kane was forced off during Bayern Munich's draw against Cologne on Friday night

Bayern confirmed the England captain is set to be out for months following the injury

But despite his injury, Kane was upbeat as he spoke to fans for the first time on Saturday (Source: Harry Kane/X)

'I'll be back fresh and ready to go for the business end of the season!

'"Tough times don't last, tough people do" - my guy (Julian Edelman) told me this one!' Kane added, quoting the NFL icon.

'Thank you for all the messages! Alles gut (red heart emoji, strong arm emoji).'

Speaking post-match, Bayern sporting director Max Eberl revealed that Kane has suffered ligament damage.

A Bayern club statement confirmed the blow, reading: 'Harry Kane suffered a tear of the syndesmosis ligament in his left ankle in Bayern Munich’s Bundesliga home match against 1. FC Köln on Friday evening.

'This was confirmed by an examination by Bayern Munich’s medical department.

'The 33-year-old will therefore be out for the next few months. Get well soon Harry.'

Kane's absence will be a major blow for Bayern, with the Ballon d'Or winner having scored ten goals in 16 matches so far this season.

Bayern, who are already without Luis Diaz, Saibari and Serge Gnabry, also saw Michael Olise come off at half-time.

The German giants later confirmed Olise had suffered a 'contusion of the forefoot', with the Frenchman set to undergo treatment in the coming days.

It remains to be seen whether Kane - who became England's most-capped player last week when making his 126th appearance in the 2-1 win over Croatia - is at risk of missing the Nations League quarter-final against Norway in March.

Vincent Kompany, who was back in the dugout for the first time since his collapse last month, admitted other players will have to step up for Bayern in the absence of star names such as Kane.

'These are world-class players, you can't replace them, but we have the mentality and we have to bring that now,' Kompany said, as per Bild.

'We need incredible performances from players who may not yet be used to this pressure at FC Bayern.

Bayern were held to a 1-1 draw by Cologne on Vincent Kompany's return to the touchline

'We will all try together to make this situation a positive one for ourselves. FC Bayern does this time and time again, and that's what we want to do now too.'

Bayern were ultimately held to a 1-1 draw by Cologne, with Tottenham loanee Mikey Moore giving the visitors a shock first half lead at the Allianz Arena.

Nathaniel Brown drew Bayern level before half-time, but the hosts were unable to find a winner on Kompany's return.

The draw gives Borussia Dortmund the chance to move to the top of the Bundesliga table on Saturday.

<a href="https://www.fiercehealthcare.com/regulatory/cms-issues-medicare-advantage-proposals-aimed-boosting-competition" hreflang="en">CMS issues Medicare Advantage proposals aimed at boosting competition</a>

📅 2026-11-20  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →
CMS issues Medicare Advantage proposals aimed at boosting competition" style="max-width:100%; height:auto; border-radius:8px;" loading="lazy">

The Trump administration has proposed a slew of changes to Medicare Advantage and Part D designed to "revitalize care" in these markets.

For one, the Centers for Medicare & Medicaid Services said late Friday that it will update application and network adequacy requirements to make it easier for new entrants. The agency proposed to require organizations to demonstrate network adequacy during their first year in operation, rather than during the application process.

This shift would eliminate a significant barrier for new entrants, driving greater competition in the market.

CMS has also proposed combining three specialty areas in behavioral health — clinical psychology, clinical social work and outpatient behavioral health — into one specialty group for the purposes of network adequacy. The changes will give plans greater flexibility in building out behavioral health networks.

CMS will also make adjustments that seek to boost competition for special needs plans.

In addition, the agency said it will make adjustments to the star ratings program, focusing its ratings on 15 measures. The proposed measure set would emphasize "clinical outcomes and patient experience while retaining select process measures in high-priority areas where performance remains low."

CMS is also aiming to update the calculation methodology that will "raise the bar for achieving high ratings," and thus quality bonus payments.

"People with Medicare should be able to choose coverage based on what matters most to them — quality, affordability, and access," said CMS Administrator Mehmet Oz, M.D., in the announcement. "These proposals are about creating a Medicare Advantage marketplace that rewards better care and better value while empowering beneficiaries with the options and knowledge they need to make informed decisions about their health."

The proposed rule also includes provisions that align with Department of Health and Human Services Secretary Robert F. Kennedy's Make America Healthy Again agenda by setting a "healthy food" standard that MA plans should follow for supplemental benefits.

The goal, CMS said, is to ensure that Medicare dollars are spent on food benefits that align with MAHA.

"Medicare food benefits should pay for nutritious food that helps people improve their health and manage chronic disease," Kennedy said in the announcement. "This proposal would require those benefits to meet the Dietary Guidelines for Americans while preserving access to foods and nutritional products that providers recommend for patients’ specific medical needs."

"Under President Trump, we are putting prevention at the center of health care and demanding more from every Medicare dollar," Kennedy said.

Toyota, Nissan, and Honda to Standardize Parts to Lower Costs

📅 Nov 20, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Japan’s automakers will work together to lower production costs as they band together to compete against China’s growing automotive sector.

Japan’s three largest automakers have agreed to begin standardizing vehicle parts in an effort to reduce costs, according to Automotive News. The outlet reports that Japan Automobile Manufacturers Association chairman Koji Sato, who is also the vice chairman of Toyota, announced the decision at a news conference in Tokyo this week.

In the short term, the standardization efforts will begin with unified quality criteria for steel, consistent protocols for plastics and resins, and a universal color-coding scheme for wiring harnesses. In the long term, the Japan Automobile Manufacturers Association has plans to move to more components that cover the entire car. The three areas were chosen as the first to be standardized because they are parts that are generally not noticed by consumers, and they shouldn’t have an impact on any brand’s individual identity. All three are also areas that account for significant production costs for the automakers, meaning they can have an immediate impact.

See all results for used Toyota for sale near 97347

The first discussions of standardization are set to include Japan’s domestic automotive parts suppliers, though international suppliers will be brought into the fold in time. The first standardization efforts are set to go into place next year, according to the AN report.

The push to simplify and lower costs comes as Japan’s seven automakers, including Toyota, Nissan, Honda, Mazda, Subaru, Mitsubishi, and Suzuki, work to fend off the increasingly competitive list of Chinese automakers. The automakers are said to hope that they can channel the money saved by homogenizing into new innovations.

“We all share this strong sense of crisis, creating a momentum for change,” Sato said. “Based upon our sense of a crisis, we decided to choose these three areas.”

Jack Fitzgerald’s love for cars stems from his as yet unshakable addiction to Formula 1. After a brief stint as a detailer for a local dealership group in college, he knew he needed a more permanent way to drive all the new cars he couldn’t afford and decided to pursue a career in auto writing. By hounding his college professors at the University of Wisconsin-Milwaukee, he was able to travel Wisconsin seeking out stories in the auto world before landing his dream job at Car and Driver. His new goal is to delay the inevitable demise of his 2010 Volkswagen Golf.
